SMARTWATCH OS AUDIT — 21 OCTOBER 2026
=====================================

100 unique findings; {'P1': 10, 'P2': 76, 'P3': 14}; 179 module/app reviews; 26 launcher apps; 70 production screens.

Coverage: 129 compiled production translation units, 227 handwritten production files / 65693 lines, 684 inventoried source/support files. All inventoried SHA256 hashes unchanged.

Read-only audit. No source fixes, firmware build/flash, reset or watch setting changes. One read-only USB diagnostic snapshot was taken. All new artifacts and private compiler temporaries stay on A:. No new worktree was needed.

PRIORITIZED REPAIR AND ACCEPTANCE PLAN
======================================

1. Protect existing data and recording recovery

Make absence distinct from I/O failure; preserve existing files until verified publication; retry unknown recording intent loads. Use collision-safe names and wait for recorder finalization before reporting success.

Related: STOR-001, STOR-002, STOR-003, STOR-004, STOR-005, HEALTH-001, APP-006, APP-007

Accept when: Inject each open/read/write/fsync/rename failure and reset between publication phases. Prove the prior valid generation/session survives and active recording resumes once the card returns. Retain SD lease, lock, checksums, alternating checkpoint and dirty retry.

2. Preserve UTC across timezone and clock changes

Apply timezone as a presentation offset without shifting UTC a second time. Return actual clock/RTC write results, retry failed synchronization and parse phone commands only at their defined top level.

Related: DEV-001, DEV-015, PROTO-001, PLAT-010, APP-013

Accept when: Change +05:30 to +06:00 and back while the picker remains open; prove UTC unchanged, calendar rollover correct and persisted RTC correct. Exercise unset RTC/calendar, rejected writes, extreme timezone day boundaries and command-like notification text.

3. Close image/update input boundaries

Enforce the image buffer/body length contract and cap every receive. Require an explicit, bounded, user-initiated update session and authorization for every firmware update entrypoint.

Related: PLAT-001, PLAT-002

Accept when: Use bounded local unit tests for zero/exact/oversized/short/aborted input and rejected update requests. Confirm authorized updates still validate the image and roll back safely. No network/device exploit was attempted in this audit.

4. Capture the first fault and survive resource pressure

Reserve/admit SD/DMA work, capture allocation owner and reset context independently of normal SD queues, and make display progress/failure observable with safe recovery.

Related: STOR-006, OBS-001, OBS-002, OBS-003, PLAT-014, PLAT-015

Accept when: Run an unplugged long-session soak with recording, screen transitions and controlled resource pressure. Retain the first error and evidence-loss counters. Validate DMA ownership before recycling buffers; an arbitrary timeout must not free a live DMA buffer.

5. Make sensor, power and app state truthful

Use monotonic sample timestamps, explicit freshness/quality, renewable foreground leases, acknowledged radio states and stable sensor-invalid UI. Preserve a Stop path through sensor loss.

Related: PLAT-003, PLAT-004, PLAT-005, PLAT-006, PLAT-007, PLAT-008, PLAT-012, PLAT-020, APP-001, APP-002, APP-003, APP-014, APP-022, HEALTH-002, HEALTH-003, HEALTH-004, HEALTH-005

Accept when: Exercise empty FIFO, conversion/read failure, absent strap, stale GPS, BLE off, SMS queue rejection and alert cover/reveal. Measure actual power while unplugged; USB rail snapshots cannot prove battery endurance.

6. Repair stable identity, clock/lifecycle and numerical boundaries

Keep stable IDs across refreshes and active edits; rebase animation time or use integer monotonic durations; make cover/reveal and time changes explicit lifecycle transitions.

Related: ENG-001, ENG-002, APP-008, APP-011, APP-012, APP-013, APP-016, APP-021, PROTO-001

Accept when: Exercise incoming notification/SMS/alarm changes during a gesture/editor, stacked alert dismissal, clock/timezone changes and long-origin animation cadence. Include high-eccentricity numerical boundary tests from the face review.

7. Gate performance, evidence and tooling together

Adopt one checked host transport, one documented validation entrypoint, build/asset/provenance/size gates, and scenario captures for every app and recording recovery path.

Related: ENG-003, ENG-004, TEST-001, HOST-001, DEV-008, DEV-009, DEV-010, DEV-011, DEV-012, DEV-013, DEV-014, DEV-016

Accept when: Keep the 30 FPS floor as a target-measured criterion (each intended present interval <=33.33 ms). Report p95/p99, misses, paint/flush/wait/asset/SD spans, cold versus warm behavior, and exact configuration. Do not replace the floor with average FPS.

EVIDENCE, PRIORITY AND SCOPE LIMITS
===================================

  - This is a targeted, read-only source audit of the current recovered dirty workspace, not a proof of absence of defects. No implementation fixes or new firmware build/flash were performed.
  - All 26 launcher entries, 70 production screen definitions and 129 compiled production translation units have coverage associations. File/entrypoint coverage does not imply every branch or screen was dynamically exercised.
  - Generated tables/fonts/art were reviewed as data, indexing, ownership and compatibility contracts, not individually verified literal values. Inactive core/apps and firmware/main/main.c are not the active OS.
  - ESP-IDF/FreeRTOS/vendor dependencies are reviewed through the project contracts and configuration used here, not as separate exhaustive audits of dependency source trees.
  - Host tests and injected positive repros validate narrow software behavior. Optional hardware, real phone protocols, unplugged battery endurance, physical sensor accuracy and power-loss/media-removal resilience remain unverified.
  - A fresh read-only USB diagnostic snapshot has no current allocation/SD log errors. Earlier low-DMA storage failures and two crash boots are separate historical evidence; exact crash causation is not established.
  - The live preference hash/brightness changed since the prior flash by user interaction (current 6f530340 / 62). The audit did not restore older settings. No device mutation was used for verification.
  - The branch HEAD predates recovered work; this report uses inventoried current files. No worktree was created or firmware substituted. All new audit/temp artifacts reside on A:.
  - Priority expresses engineering urgency for this early-stage system. P0 means demonstrated critical emergency (none confirmed); P1 means address before trusting data/update boundaries; P2 means functional/reliability improvement; P3 means lower-impact correctness/documentation/observability debt.

APP-BY-APP REPORT — ALL LAUNCHER ENTRIES
========================================

Activity [scr_activity]

Source: nos/apps/fit_activity.c:546 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: ACTIVITY steps/goal page [health_sports:app.activity.hero]

First of three vertical pages, showing daily steps and goal progress.

Goal comes from preference/default8000; prepared per-frame values prevent stripe/damage inconsistency. Entry/reveal preserves page and built hero, unlike Energy. Vertical pager owns only appropriate drags; widget shares data but separate hero. Screen can show zero when underlying activity is unavailable because public activity display lacks a persistent quality banner; zero is not proof of verified inactivity.

No additional confirmed defect in this scope. See module limits below.

Sports [scr_workout]

Source: nos/apps/fit_workout.c:1003 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: SPORTS chooser, live session and result summary [health_sports:app.sports.main]

One app with choose/live/summary views backed by background workout snapshots.

Favorites prioritize football, cricket, badminton, running and hiking; sixth tile opens all sports. Live controls give LAP/PAUSE and paused FINISH/RESUME; leaving does not pause. Monotonic active time and missing-value labels are preserved, views read snapshots rather than file I/O. Result shows SAVING/SAVED/FAILED, retry and retained-memory status; unsaved result survives screen dismissal and blocks new start. Hardware button/press ownership uses shared app input conventions and bounded feedback state; no background per-frame worker or file I/O is added by drawing.

Findings: HEALTH-001

Energy [scr_energy]

Source: nos/apps/fit_energy.c:268 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: ENERGY history, food/water editors and undo [health_sports:app.energy]

Dated activity-burn estimate plus checked manual intake ledger.

Async24bin queries clear old bins and report loading/unavailable; drag zoom uses a declared minute range, tap selects bars and footer resets day zoom. Food/water editor clamps1..10000 and submits checked intake; undo uses explicit ledger API. BURN is cumulative step activity estimate, EAT/WATER per interval, not a metabolic balance or total daily energy; footer states ACTIVITY CALORIES ONLY. Root reviews ledger persistence, queues and I/O failure semantics in STOR-001..007.

Findings: HEALTH-006

Battery history [scr_battery_history]

Source: nos/apps/fit_energy.c:269 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: BATTERY HISTORY voltage, level and mode timeline [health_sports:app.battery_history]

Dated valid battery/voltage bins with current power-source context.

Selected battery bins require valid sample counts and say NO VALID SAMPLE when absent. Voltage and percent are separate views; modes/external power markers provide context. Top power rail/source comes from current HAL, even when dated chart is historical.80MHz idle toggle updates user preference. Battery percentages are the platform voltage-model estimate, not fuel-gauge measured capacity. STOR-007 owns retry duplicate weighting; platform owns rail/percent validity and calibration limitations.

Findings: HEALTH-006

Review: Battery level and top-menu route [developer_tooling:battery]

Control Center header and Settings Battery show the current qualified rail-derived power state.

Header route uses static BT state, svc_sys and cached hal_power; it performs no app-level SD history query or malloc/free. Unknown/USB-settling levels show --, source/rail age is stated. Re-entry overwrites the small snapshot and no reveal-owned pointer is retained. Quick Tools Battery History is a different screen in fit_energy and its SD query belongs to health/storage review. No exact physical crash cause can be proven here from old logs without a fault PC/backtrace.

No additional confirmed defect in this scope. See module limits below.

Sensor logs [scr_sports_logs]

Source: nos/apps/fit_energy.c:270 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: SENSOR LOGS / RAW SENSORS dated event browser [health_sports:app.sensor_logs]

Browse archived/recent/raw sports records and field labels.

Unlike chart queries, performs synchronous sports_archived/sports_raw reads in UI tick once a second (sports.c479-509), including SD lock and file scanning; slow storage or long invalid-record scans can stall frames/input. This does not identify an exact crash cause. It switches raw/events, pages four of eight fields and navigates date/offset. Header exposes storage availability/lost/error counts; report labels decode feature/outcome and power modes. Missing/no-more events are not a proof of no physical activity. Timestamp/day+uptime enables partial correlation; root owns archive/raw queue correctness and failure persistence.

Findings: HEALTH-006

Wrist tuning [scr_wrist_tuning]

Source: nos/apps/fit_energy.c:272 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: WRIST TUNING thresholds, trials and learned view [health_sports:app.wrist_tuning]

Manual walking/knock thresholds and labeled gesture/view-learning trials.

Threshold/entry-peaks/quiet-time controls use tuning_adjust checked persistence. Trial feature spans walking/knock/raise/pickup/turn/air/touch/GPS/recorder; expected10/20/50 repetitions are manual labels, not sensed success counts. Raise/pickup/turn start wake view learning and show its result. trial_started survives child navigation in static RAM, but not reset; trial markers are best-effort sports_emit and have no durable start/end acknowledgement. Reset false success and reveal reset are specific bugs. This screen is not the bilateral watch-wrist detector.

Findings: HEALTH-006, HEALTH-007

Quick report [scr_quick_report]

Source: nos/apps/fit_energy.c:271 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: QUICK REPORT feature/outcome/time labels [health_sports:app.quick_report]

Manual timestamp marker and feature/outcome label for physical validation.

Ten feature categories, three outcome classes and30/60/300second windows; a just-ended tuning trial seeds feature/duration. Initial open adds an unlabelled marker, explicit SAVE LABEL writes outcome via checked sports_report. Labels help reproducibility but are user assertions rather than an automatic ground truth sensor. They do not encode sport shot counts, racket hand or scorer annotations.

Findings: HEALTH-006

Heart rate [scr_heart]

Source: nos/apps/fit_heart.c:921 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: HEART RATE live/contact/locked/absent page [health_sports:app.heart.live]

HR strap present/absent, contact acquisition, PPG trace and locked BPM/SpO2.

Explicit absent/no-contact/measuring/locked states and reveal rearm preserve a reading across sheets. Optional PSRAM dot lists gracefully draw nothing if allocation fails; memory is reused rather than leaked every enter. While measuring keeps awake, then allows normal sleep20seconds after lock. PPG wave visualization is not ECG. Presence, freshness and lock-value clearing are conflated as finding002; stream owner conflict finding005. The strap is not an onboard capability on every board.

Findings: HEALTH-002, HEALTH-005

Breathe [scr_breathe]

Source: nos/apps/fit_breathe.c:552 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: BREATHE start, intro, session, outro, summary and again [health_sports:app.breathe]

Guided1/3/5minute sessions with4s inhale,2s hold,6s exhale and optional HR average.

All six internal states accounted: START duration choice, INTRO setup, SESSION cue/haptic loop, OUTRO close, SUMMARY minutes/breath count/optionalHR, SUMOUT→INTRO for AGAIN; DONE leaves. Keeps awake during running/intro, optional haptics obey preferences. Cover/reveal retains session state and reenables HR; session progression follows visible UI dt and stops while this screen is not ticked, rather than recording an independent wall-clock workout. Displayed breaths are planned cycles, not measured respiration. HR sampling flaw and shared disable can mislead averages/interrupt workout. No durable breathing history or clinical stress/respiration inference.

Findings: HEALTH-003, HEALTH-005

Weather [scr_weather]

Source: nos/apps/out_weather.c:824 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: Weather app and home widget [content_apps:out.weather]

Current conditions, hourly/daily panels, animated conditions and optional on-wrist temperature.

Reviewed Wi-Fi/phone/no-feed/connecting states, bounded source snapshots, retries, metric/imperial temperature/wind, unknown humidity/wind placeholders, forecast count caps, city title and data swaps, icon/graph range math, vertical pager, widget shared frame guard, and optional environmental temperature. HTTP body/parser are bounded and malformed forecasts are rejected before HAL publication. Service conversion loses freshness and forecast timestamps.

Real service arithmetic handles ordinary uptime wrap; normal UI durations are well below signed deadline limits. Ring stack cleanup is the confirmed interruption issue.

IMU [scr_imu]

Source: nos/apps/out_imu.c:180 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: IMU LIVE filtered cache and graph [health_sports:app.imu.live]

Diagnostic acceleration/gyro preview, tilt dot and history traces.

Lazy UI allocation checked and retains state across visits. Samples cached hal_imu_read from UI, not the independent fixed-rate raw recorder; shows unavailable when HAL read fails. Per-axis preview clips to±2g and±120dps even when sensor hardware range is larger. Hashes chronological pixels to avoid unnecessary repaint at rest. Timing label has no timestamp backing. This preview cannot validate500Hz raw sensor delivery or count sports technique.

Diagnostics [scr_diag]

Source: nos/apps/set_diag.c:1366 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: Diagnostics: guided cards, result list and raw rows [developer_tooling:diagnostics]

User-guided button/touch/haptic/speaker checks and15 automatic/optional health results.

All15 test definitions and result transitions inspected. Results are bounded static records; four guided slots always include board button/touch. True exit aborts streams and restores card changes; cover preserves running state and reveal reasserts demand. Read values are cached, so repeated getter calls do not establish sensor acquisition rate. The light frozen verdict is demonstrably a false positive. RTC test observes system-clock progression, not independent PCF85063 readback; SD test is mount/capacity plausibility, not a durability write test.

Source: nos/apps/set_settings.c:1804 | coverage association: entrypoint

Review: Settings and all internal controls [developer_tooling:settings]

Active preferences UI, native retained rows and subpages; hardware/persistence callers followed where behavior depends on them.

Reviewed every row group and internal screen, picker/slider press-drag-release, live preference writes, optional-module labels, reset/restart dispatch, cache allocation rollback/reveal and learning completion. Sliders apply hardware and clear/reapply AOD preview across lifecycle; dirty prefs use shared retry service. Concrete timezone, GPS, wrist and false clock-set success defects are separate from shared storage checkpoint findings. Reset settings is preferences reset, not data/factory erase; separate wake_cfg/alarms/history persistence is outside its current promise.

SPORTS PROFILE AUDIT — ALL 40 STABLE IDS
========================================

These are local category assumptions and shared algorithms, not 40 validated sport recognizers. MET values were checked against the local research document, not independently verified externally. Hand and wrist metadata do not make generic motion a technique classifier.

0 | WALK
========

Family: outdoor_locomotion | MET category: 3.8

Source: nos/workout.c:56

Local reference: {"profile": "WALK", "met": 3.8, "reference_code": "17190", "flags": "L", "assumption": "Level moderate walk", "line": 203}

Available outputs:

  - monotonic active/total timer
  - manual laps
  - quality-gated external HR with coverage
  - generic wrist movement coverage/bursts
  - gross category energy only with entered mass
  - external GNSS accepted distance and valid-time average
  - pace presentation
  - activity counter delta and cadence

Withheld or missing:

  - automatic sport recognition
  - validated sporting accuracy
  - per-session GPS route geometry
  - clinical energy/HR claims

Validation: shared engine host contracts only; no new sport-specific physical validation

MODULE-BY-MODULE REPORT — ROUTINES, LOGGING AND DEVELOPER GAPS
==============================================================

Frame engine and animation clock [engine:engine]
================================================

Role: Own the UI-thread frame, input/service/tick sequence, elapsed interval and published renderer statistics.

Assessment: Finite, positive elapsed input is capped to four periods and propagated consistently to tick and exposure. The existing irregular-cadence regression passes. A large animation-clock origin reproducibly loses fractional periods and eventually stops advancing. The documented whole-period contract is stale.

Files: nos/nos.c, nos/nos.h, nos/render_config.h

Findings: ENG-002, ENG-005

Entry points/routines:

  - nos_init
  - nos_frame_run

Logging gaps:

  - No direct clock-precision/large-origin alarm; performance counters show dt but do not report nos_t stagnation.

Developer / feature gaps:

  - Add long-origin cases to the engine clock regression.

Checks:

  - 31-test host group
  - existing real-engine clock regression
  - current-engine large-origin reproducer

Coverage limits:

  - Read-only source review and host execution; target timing, physical touch, optical appearance and prolonged device operation were not measured in this audit.
  - The large-origin test injects the clock value; it does not simulate six days of uninterrupted operation.

Host power policy and shutdown orchestration [platform:power_host]
==================================================================

Role: Owns ON/AOD/OFF/SENSOR transitions, brightness, wake input, low-battery policy, explicit restart/deep sleep.

Assessment: State changes are UI-owned. Low battery has 15/20 percent hysteresis, preserves user preferences, suppresses motion/knock wake while allowing touch/button. OFF sleeps the panel and samples services periodically; AOD paints at 1Hz after its entry animation; SENSOR caps CPU at 80MHz after entry. Ordinary display-off leaves sensing/recording active. Shutdown flushes prefs and cancels assets, with an IMU-recording guard, but lacks complete external-stream coordination. There is no automatic light sleep, current measurement or fuel gauge.

Files: firmware/main/nos_main.c, nos/power_policy.h

Findings: PLAT-006, PLAT-007, PLAT-012, PLAT-013, PLAT-014, PLAT-015

Entry points/routines:

  - app_main
  - go_state
  - nos_host_power
  - hal_display_power
  - auto_brightness_tick
  - power_low_update

Logging gaps:

  - Power-state events exist; requested/applied panel transitions and external stop acknowledgments do not.
  - No measured time/current cost per power state or attached-module combination.

Developer / feature gaps:

  - Expose why sleep is blocked, wake-source timestamps, acquired power leases and actual effective CPU/radio/module state in one screen.

Checks:

  - power_policy_test via Python passes; PM lock/fallback tests pass.

Coverage limits:

  - Battery/runtime effects and strap shutdown currents unmeasured; root reviews active workout/recorder persistence interactions.

Touch sampling, queue and wake swallowing [platform:touch]

Assessment: Ring snapshots use a spinlock and preserve last coordinates on release. Waking touch is swallowed until release. Ring overflow drops oldest input silently.

Onboard IMU and raw recorder worker adapter [platform:imu_platform]
===================================================================

Role: Configures +/-8g +/-512dps500Hz QMI8658, feeds knock/workout/wrist and smoothed cache; separate25/50/100Hz raw sampler/timer and SD writer task.

Assessment: Raw sampler owns a sample mutex and skips missed slots rather than issuing catch-up reads; stream tracks missed/errors. Creation mutex prevents worker reentry before handles publish. Task stacks allocate through tracked PSRAM; small TCBs stay internal. Cached pipeline uses ~3Hz software smoothing and board-task acquisition, so it has different behavior/latency from raw recorder. 500Hz sensor ODR is not the board polling rate and should not be claimed as delivered cache sampling.

Files: firmware/main/imu_hal.c, firmware/main/board.c, nos/imu_backend.h

Findings: PLAT-009, PLAT-013

  - board_imu_raw/read

  - imu_backend_wake/start/stop
  - sample_task
  - write_task

  - Silent board raw/knock read failures and unavailable flag lack per-operation counters/reprobe history.
  - No sampler deadline maximum, I2C wait attribution or complete task-create stage failure reason.

Developer / feature gaps:

  - Report configured sensor ODR, delivered cache/raw rates, time since sample, task/stack ownership and error/loss counters distinctly.

Checks:

  - Motion/core portable tests pass; root owns recorder/storage tests.

Coverage limits:

  - No physical rate/axis/noise/interrupt/overrun validation. Public core HAL limits rates before the backend; backend itself assumes nonzero supported hz.

Daily walking totals and core cadence detector [platform:pedometer]
===================================================================

Role: Allocation-free conservative coherent-peak walking detector and local-date daily step/active-time accumulation.

Assessment: No confirmed portable algorithm defect in reviewed paths. Rejects nonfinite/implausible motion, duplicate timestamps, >100ms gaps and incoherent peak cadence; saturates step counters; day rollover discards previous candidate bout. Daily totals save at most once/minute when background persistence is allowed and are retried on later eligible cycles after failure. Sampling pauses can lose candidates; this is conservative, not validated fitness output.

Files: core/pedometer.c, core/pedometer.h, core/walk.c, core/walk.h

Raise-to-wake model and learned pose persistence [platform:motion_wake]
=======================================================================

Role: Fresh-sample gesture state machine, stillness/gyro bias, pickup/turn arms, reading-cone hold, checksum pose configuration.

Assessment: No confirmed new defect in portable state machine. Config is checksummed/range-validated; setting changes are applied only after checked save. Learning requires still, finite samples and times out after10s. Repeated cached timestamp is ignored; invalid samples/gaps reset state. Eligibility respects lock/low-battery/prefs. Behavior is heuristic and depends on delivered smoothed samples.

Double-knock gesture [platform:knock]

External module service and shared contracts [platform:ext_system]
==================================================================

Role: Probes optional devices, allocates PSRAM state, serializes cached samples and runs the single priority0 service loop.

Assessment: Optional absence generally does not abort boot; I2C driver serializes bus transactions. C-board strap pins are intentionally disabled. Most init probes are one-time. Cached snapshot access is cheap but service control flags/lease timestamps often cross tasks without a common publication protocol. Task-create failure only prints a warning and leaves several presence bits set; attached hardware readiness is not fully represented.

  - Extracted actual lease helper check reproduces PLAT-003.

  - No attached strap was probed during audit; all electrical/timing claims derive from source and need hardware qualification.

BLE discovery, phone transport and media snapshots [platform:ble]
=================================================================

Role: NimBLE peripheral/NUS phone link, leased active discovery, minimal accessory central connection, time/location and media relay.

Assessment: Discovery low duty cycle avoids continuous scanning; table is mutex-protected and old entries age out. UI NUS reads take a zero-timeout lock; complete lines queue bounded in PSRAM and overlong lines drop. NUS transmit retries can delay UI per chunk under mbuf pressure, so nonblocking getters do not imply all HAL calls are nonblocking. Accessory connection only establishes BLE link; there is no audio/A2DP support. Radio does not honor the apparent Bluetooth off preference. Fixed static random identity is shared by builds, limiting multiple identical watches.

Active LCD/QSPI flush and panel state [platform:display]
========================================================

Role: Two internal raw stripes, two rotated DMA buffers, core1 ordered flush jobs, DCS commands and frame/exposure fences.

Assessment: Ownership is explicit: raw credit returns after final rotation read; rotated credit returns only on DMA callback; final ordered fence drains both. Exposure metadata has same raw lifetime. Exact paired rotation is guarded by alignment/even-height fallback. This is a DMA-to-panel-GRAM fence, not vblank/optical presentation. Initialization uses a working CO5300 profile without claiming confirmed controller identity. Unbounded DMA waits and ignored DCS errors limit recovery.

Navigate NOW, TRACK and BACK TO START [content_apps:out.nav]
============================================================

Role: GPS status/live values, background in-RAM route recording, GPX export and return-to-start guidance.

Assessment: Reviewed optional GPS probing, satellite fields, fix loss/reacquisition, 30-second retention, hold-to-stop/input hit guards, background recorder demand, first/reopened session, 4096-point halving, 360-dot visualization, projection/scale/array bounds, current marker, heading-source fallback, distance units, GPX enumeration/write result, and session restart. No false no-fix arrival claim was filed: nav_draw_body explicitly shows WAITING FOR FIX if no last fix has ever existed. Confirmed issues concern recovery after real fixes, Stop access, and saving failure.

Files: nos/apps/out_nav.c

  - TRACK loc_sync/rebuild_track_view/you_place and bounded resampling
  - BACK hav_m/bearing_deg/arrow_step and source selection
  - svc_track_*

Logging gaps:

  - No fix accept/reject reason, outage age, last anchor, session save result/path/error, or reanchor counter.
  - Allocation failure silently leaves no active track/view and the Start action still reports toggle feedback.

Developer / feature gaps:

  - [feature_gap] No on-watch saved-track library, GPX import/routes/turn-by-turn navigation or retry/export UI. Active routes are RAM-only; reset/power loss loses unsaved points.
  - [feature_gap] Distance and altitude remain metres/km on track/back screens under imperial preference even though NOW speed honors mph.
  - [unverified_hardware_risk] BACK compares a geodesic true-north bearing with the HAL's magnetic heading, with no declination correction; exact directional error depends on location and sensor calibration. Heading/frame convention must be verified on hardware.
  - [edge_case_limit] Local track projection does not unwrap longitude at the antimeridian; worldwide/polar/very long routes are not a bounded validated use case.
  - [cross_reference] GPX writes run synchronously on the UI thread, enumerate up to 9999 files, omit mount lease/SD lock and ignore fclose errors. Root storage/platform review owns common SD lifecycle/durability findings.

Checks:

  - build/full-os-audit-20261021/checks/content/track_reacquisition.log
  - build/full-os-audit-20261021/checks/content/navigation_fix_loss.log
  - Full svc_track code, GPS stale getter, app draw guards, global stop callers and SD save paths reviewed.

Coverage limits:

  - No GPS receiver, outdoor movement, magnetometer, real SD GPX/fault or 4096-point endurance test.
  - Extreme geometry/NaN NMEA input and route projection performance are code-reviewed only.

SEGMENT [faces:face_segment]

Face registry and shared helpers [faces:face_registry_helpers]
==============================================================

Role: Nine-face registry, optional input/hits/styles, time/date formatting, wake/AOD, dot caches, scaled fonts and damage helpers.

Assessment: Registry contains all nine actual faces including Orrery. Input overrides are limited to Orrery; ordinary complications route to declared screens. Bounded copies protect morph/xfade strings. PSRAM dot lists are process-lifetime optional storage and directly evaluate if unavailable. The dot cache intentionally only attempts allocation once; a transient failure permanently loses this optimization until reboot. Shared date fade still uses 30 Hz despite a 40 Hz configured cadence. Draw helpers honor recording versus raster paths and clipping.

ORRERY / Solar face [faces:face_orrery]
=======================================

Role: Interactive solar-system face, hold/lock ownership, view/pan/zoom/time controls and card/events entry.

Assessment: Locked and unlocked input guards, edge ownership, closing-animation ownership, button locking, hold thresholds, AOD reset and cache trim were read. Astronomical/layout/command preparation is primarily tick-owned and striped replay has a direct fallback. The main state is process-lifetime PSRAM; no basic clock is drawn if it cannot be obtained. Time travel has no declared model-range guard. Asteroid/inner-small-body motion is deliberately sliced; exact release is distinguished from transient previews. Menu filters are implemented, with Earth intentionally retained. Current AOD omits the unread indicator and Orrery has no shared low-battery complication; these are feature gaps rather than additional asserted crashes.

Target-exported asteroid basis [faces:orrery_basis]
===================================================

Role: 3999 target-rounded 13-float asteroid basis records, CRC export and runtime validation/backend selection.

Assessment: Asset source SHA256 and decoded 3999x13 IEEE32 layout match provenance. Runtime validation uses acquire/release publication and rejects record or float-bit mismatches; unready/rejected tables select runtime calculation. Copy and flash modes differ in ownership; face-owned copy is retained for process lifetime, flash is borrowed. Host target-bit rejection is an intended possibility. The metadata status still says rebuild verification pending, while later dated device documentation records activation; this stale status should not substitute for the documented firmware identity.

Files: nos/faces/orrery_basis.h, nos/faces/orrery_basis_crc.h, nos/faces/assets/orrery_basis_target.inc, nos/faces/assets/orrery_basis_target.json

Findings: no additional confirmed defect in this review scope.

Entry points/routines:

  - orrery_basis_check/ready/mode/dump/dates_check; belt_init

Logging gaps:

  - Compact face state does not disclose requested versus actual basis backend; explicit basis diagnostics do.

Developer / feature gaps:

  - Reconcile provenance status with the later immutable activation record without replacing the capture hashes.

Checks:

Ephemerides and event scanner [faces:ephem]
===========================================

Role: Double-precision planet/conic/satellite/moon geometry and resumable event scan.

Assessment: Confirmed unsafeguarded elliptic Kepler failure for actual bundled comet input. Planet p/body indices are trusted internal API inputs; reviewed current callers constrain them. Generic zero-vector ep_observe produces undefined angular quantities for Earth/Sun, but inspected card callers override or do not display those fields, so no separate reachable user defect is asserted. Card allocations and asynchronous assets [faces:card_assets]
============================================================

Role: Transactional reusable PSRAM storage, paged texture transfers, generation adoption and exit/reentry reap.

Assessment: Confirmed asset failure is terminal until a body reset, with PHOTO still selected. Destination page pointers move to the backend and are unavailable to render/free until take; stale/cancelled generations are freed rather than adopted. Release waits for outgoing transitions and noncovered leave; pending backend pages remain backend-owned. Mandatory card allocation failure is explicitly displayed as MEMORY UNAVAILABLE and a later open can retry. Photo texture allocation remains mandatory even though untextured shading exists, a product/performance tradeoff rather than an extra asserted crash.

Files: nos/faces/orrery_card.c

  - allocate/release_buffers/load_begin/load_tick/orrery_card_asset_poll/idle/enter/leave

  - CARDRESULT counts failures but does not retain failing path, read length/error cause, retry eligibility or user-visible missing-card/missing-file distinction.

Developer / feature gaps:

  - Offer retry/SD-ready recovery and distinguish PHOTO fallback; avoid keeping a failed 1 MiB asset allocation when memory is needed elsewhere.

Checks:

  - card-asset-retry-repro: missing Sun file reaches stage3/tex0; valid 1 MiB file arriving afterward is never submitted.
  - Existing delayed-backend ownership oracle was read, not rerun.

Coverage limits:

  - No SD card, COM3, actual IO cancellation latency or physical memory-fragmentation test.

Card progressive rendering and motion proxy [faces:card_progressive]
====================================================================

Role: Cooperative back-sprite construction, immutable front publish, exact-at-rest/coarse motion geometry and bounded proxy.

Assessment: No independently confirmed new publish/row-buffer defect. Front/back ownership is explicit; jobs snapshot body/mode/free/assets and cancel on generation changes. Geometry and rendering have row/quanta caps; hardware time budget is checked between bounded work units, so budget_us is not a strict worst-quantum deadline. Proxy only runs within 5-degree spin/3-degree latitude/200ms limits; outside it deliberately retains a stale complete front. Detail=2 or4 is motion-only; rest returns to1. These choices can reduce image fidelity/pose response during motion and must be measured separately from frame throughput.

Files: nos/faces/orrery_card_progressive.inc, nos/faces/orrery_card_proxy.inc, nos/faces/orrery_card_rows.inc

Checks:

  - Prepared-state oracle rerun; row and publish/cancel callers inspected; existing progressive/proxy fixtures read.

Multiple almost-identical shader functions increase maintenance risk; exact helper tests help but do not establish physical lighting accuracy. Workout state machine, time and boundaries [health_sports:workout.core]
=======================================================================

Role: Background-owned session state independent of the Sports screen.

Assessment: Uses monotonic uint32 millisecond deltas, not wall-clock differences, so clock/timezone corrections do not change elapsed exercise. Running elapsed excludes manual pauses; total includes observed pause time but excludes reset downtime. Wrap arithmetic, duplicate boundary polls, bounded20laps and12history entries are tested. Format caps time at30days; no indefinite multi-month guarantee or automatic stop at that cap. Elapsed service gaps are counted as observed running time while sensor coverage clears across gaps. Closing UI leaves workout active; pause/discard are explicit.

Workout GNSS freshness, distance, speed and pace [health_sports:workout.gnss]
=============================================================================

Standing Doppler gate<0.55m/s rejects most position noise; display speed is low-pass while position distance is independent. Alternating workout banks, load/recovery and writer resources [health_sports:workout.persistence]
================================================================================================

Role: Separate worker stores complete bounded session/history with checksums and lineage.

Assessment: Worker polls500ms, periodic running checkpoints30seconds and failure retry5seconds; explicit boundary revisions coalesce. SD mount lease/lock, checked fwrite/flush/fsync/close/readback and exact bank identity protect normal writes. Save acknowledgement only clears current revision; pending/failed results remain in RAM and block a new session. V1 migrates with checksum and paused recovery; V2 freezes opt-in resume, excludes reboot downtime, resets live anchors and logs recovery. Corrupt bank pairs and foreign readable banks are preserved. Read-EIO uncertainty and one-shot resource initialization are exceptions below. Maximum power-loss exposure is the interval since last successful checkpoint, which can exceed30seconds under sustained failures; no atomic-history miracle is claimed.

Files: nos/workout.c, nos/workout.h

Findings: HEALTH-001, HEALTH-010, HEALTH-011, HEALTH-014

Developer / feature gaps:

  - Maintain explicit load/IO/worker status and a safe export/repair workflow that preserves original banks.

Checks:

Daily activity state, rollover and persistence [health_sports:service.activity]
===============================================================================

Role: Publish daily steps, heuristic distance/calories/active minutes and rolling/hourly totals.

Assessment: On device reads actual activity cache rather than simulator fixtures. Distance uses fixed0.75m/step; calories fixed0.04kcal/step, independent of user mass or workout MET. Active minute means>60step delta per observed minute, stand-hours field means>250hourly steps, not standing/posture sensing. Counter decreases baseline safely; clock day changes shift rolling history, backward/>7day jumps clear a week. Daily minutely timing is local-clock based and history/last steps can reset on day correction; no clinical activity score is justified. Raw step state is owned by platform/pedometer; nosact persistence failure handling is independently defective.

Energy/BatteryHistory asynchronous query memory and lock ownership [health_sports:history.query_backend]
================================================================================================

Role: Preallocated worker jobs and fixed24bin results used by Energy/BatteryHistory.

Assessment: fit_energy E.bins is static24element storage, not a per-open cache/index allocation. On device requested/working query jobs/results are in PSRAM-allocated s_journal (sports.c246); allocation failure returns before ready. Request requires ready and writer_task (364-366), so task creation failure yields HISTORY UNAVAILABLE instead of synchronous fallback. UI request snapshots retained intake IDs under critical section; polling copies only a completed matching token under lock. Worker copies new request at400, acquires SD lease/lock at403, processes<=128records per step and releases at432 before publishing. Obsolete job results cannot replace a newer token at439. No query filesystem work or SD lock occurs in fit_energy chart enter/tick/draw. Synchronous sports_query447 is a distinct API and fit_energy does not call it; SensorLogs does call synchronous archived/raw APIs. No confirmed chart memory corruption or race from these paths. Settings top-menu Battery-health is a separate path reviewed by parent/dev agent; historical crash cause is unknown without PC/backtrace and must not be attributed to prior SD DMA failure.

Files: nos/sports.c, nos/sports.h, nos/apps/fit_energy.c

Findings: no additional confirmed defect in this review scope.

Entry points/routines:

  - sports_init
  - sports_query_request
  - sports_query_poll
  - query_step
  - writer

Logging gaps:

  - Three-second lease acquisition timeout and generic HISTORY UNAVAILABLE lack operation/errno and acquisition timeline; root OBS-001 records missing crash PC/backtrace.

Developer / feature gaps:

  - Expose worker initialization status, query token/progress/lock-wait age and a captured fault breadcrumb.

Checks:

  - Source allocation/guard/token/SD-lock tracing; root owns sports query storage/fault tests.

Coverage limits:

  - No new Battery-screen crash attempted or target query run. Shared host simulation uses different allocation/thread paths.

COMPLETE UNIQUE FINDINGS
========================

APP-006 | P1 | Recorder file naming can overwrite an existing recording
=======================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Start a recording at a local month/day/hour/minute/second already used by an existing memo: for example after a manual clock rollback, timezone/DST clock repetition, next year, or two short starts in the same second after finalization.

Impact: The path contains no year, monotonic sequence, or collision suffix. The HAL opens it with wb, truncating the existing recording before any new samples are captured.

Recommended change: Allocate a unique persistent filename with full UTC date and a collision suffix or sequence; create it exclusively and retry on existence. Retain the full recording timestamp as metadata for chronological sorting and today labels.

Verification/limits: method: Direct application filename and actual HAL open mode inspection; no existing user files were touched.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/con_memo.c:429 — The recording filename is M_MMDD_HHMMSS.wav, without year or uniqueness test.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_audio.c:503 — The start guard checks only microphone/path/active state, not an existing file.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_audio.c:504 — fopen(path, wb) truncates an existing recording.
  - nos/apps/con_memo.c:129 — Month/day filename sorting also lacks year information.

DEV-001 | P1 | Leaving the timezone picker changes UTC by the timezone delta again
==================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Open Settings > Time zone, change +05:30 to +06:00, let at least the next normal firmware loop run, then back out or receive a covering alert.

Impact: pick_commit changes the preference while the picker remains open. The host applies the new TZ before the exit; pick_leave then adds another 30 minutes and hal_set_datetime changes UTC by that amount. Larger selections can shift hours or dates; alarms, calendar/day grouping, journal epochs and filenames can become wrong. board_clock_changed queues this erroneous epoch for RTC persistence when the RTC write succeeds, so a reboot need not repair it. This is separate from world-clock display APP-013 and RTC write retry PLAT-010.

Recommended change: Keep UTC unchanged when selecting a display offset. Apply the offset once in the host; remove wall-clock mutation from picker exit and compute previews from a UTC anchor. Use calendar conversion instead of hand-written one-month rollover. Add firmware-like timezone/epoch invariance checks, including quarter-hour and date-boundary choices.

Verification/limits: timezone_picker.log: actual picker exit with firmware-already-applied +06:00 wall11:00 sets11:30. Host/HAL/RTC chain inspected; physical RTC not exercised.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/set_settings.c:1853 — Picker immediately changes tz_min; prefs_save is called before exit.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:1508 — Active loop applies changed preference to g_tz_offset_min and libc TZ.
  - nos/apps/set_settings.c:1881 — Exit reads the already shifted local datetime, adds the old-to-new offset, then calls hal_set_datetime at1895. Preview1839 also adds the delta again.
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:118 — mktime interprets the supplied wall time in the new timezone; settimeofday129 changes epoch and board_clock_changed130 queues RTC write.
  - firmware/main/board.c:455 — Pending epoch is consumed by RTC save461; restore493 decodes UTC at next boot.
  - sim/nos_hal_sim.c:164 — Simulator wall RTC is separate from sim_tz_min; it does not reproduce the firmware host preference application.

HEALTH-001 | P1 | Unreadable latest workout bank can be overwritten by an older recovered checkpoint
================================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: A previously valid latest workouts.N.bin returns a non-ENOENT read failure while the other, older bank is readable; that latest bank remains unreadable through the next checkpoint preflight but accepts a write. Recovery of the older active or paused session itself requests a checkpoint.

Impact: Recovery silently rolls back to the older session/history, reports ready/available with zero errors, then may destroy the only newer checkpoint. The foreign-card hash guard cannot identify a bank that could not be read. This is the workout recorder analogue of STOR-001; it is a separate call chain and file format.

Recommended change: Keep absent, validated-corrupt and unreadable states distinct. Defer authoritative recovery and all destructive bank writes while any existing bank has an unresolved I/O error; retry reads and retain an error/slot/errno. Preserve recovery lineage until both banks can be assessed.

Verification/limits: checks/health/workout_read_eio.json: actual workout.c and existing host test stubs, audit-only fopen EIO on latest bank reads until its first wb. Before: latest bank0 elapsed2000, bank1 elapsed1000. Reboot loads1000 ready1 available1 errors0; subsequent recovery save writes bank0 elapsed1000. Compile and assertion-backed observation pass; no device faults injected.

Source evidence:

  - nos/workout.c:475 — read_slot_status returns -1 for non-ENOENT fopen failure, read error, close failure or invalid contents, collapsing transient I/O uncertainty and confirmed invalidity.
  - nos/workout.c:505 — newest_slot chooses the readable bank even if the other state is -1; only neither valid yields -2.
  - nos/workout.c:661 — The load path accepts slot>=0 and marks ok true at 673, learning identities only for readable banks.
  - nos/workout.c:628 — Recovered active/paused sessions immediately call checkpoint_request at 634.
  - nos/workout.c:533 — write_checkpoint derives a/b from successful reads only; foreign guard at 538 inspects only valid readable banks.
  - nos/workout.c:555 — With one readable bank, target=1-latest; fopen(path,"wb") at 562 truncates the unreadable other bank.

PLAT-001 | P1 | HTTP cover reception can exceed its allocated image buffer
==========================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: A cover request declares a body larger than the fixed 250 x 250 RGB565 image.

Impact: The handler can write beyond its 125000-byte PSRAM allocation; memory corruption is a correctness risk independently of who sends the request. The station web server exposes the handler after association. No device or network request was made during this audit.

Recommended change: Reject content_len values other than the required image size before allocation or reception; bound each receive by remaining image capacity and publish only a complete staged image.

Verification/limits: Source-proven bound mismatch. Root owns independent defensive review; no reproduction tooling or traffic was used.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1378 — Fixed need = COVER_PX*COVER_PX*2; allocation uses exactly need.
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1384 — Loop checks got < need, but recv at line 1385 requests total-got rather than remaining allocation capacity.
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1465 — Cover handler is registered independently of setup_authorized.
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1610 — Station GOT_IP starts the server.

PLAT-002 | P1 | Firmware update routes lack the explicit setup-session gate
===========================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: The watch joins a station network and a client reaches its HTTP update routes.

Impact: A valid application image can be installed without a watch-side update session or application-layer authorization. Image structural integrity and rollback are separate from authorizing an update. This source finding is preserved for the root reviewer; no update was attempted.

Recommended change: Require an explicit bounded update session initiated on the watch and an authorization check shared by every update entrypoint; qualify a signed-image policy if authenticated firmware is required.

Verification/limits: Source and checked-in configuration review only. Root independently verifies reachability and intended deployment trust boundary.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1610 — Server starts on station GOT_IP.
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1481 — OTA routes are registered on that server.
  - firmware/main/ota.c:106 — Upload handler only checks the busy flag and creates the worker.
  - firmware/main/ota.c:146 — URL handler accepts a bounded URL body and starts the worker without the setup-session gate.
  - firmware/main/ota.c:221 — All three update routes register unconditionally.
  - firmware/sdkconfig:494 — Secure boot is not configured.
  - firmware/sdkconfig:1387 — HTTP download is permitted by the OTA client configuration.

STOR-001 | P1 | A transient checkpoint read error can destroy the newest previously durable preferences
================================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: storage_services

Trigger: Newest-slot fopen/read fails transiently; save treats it as absent, selects the older slot as newest, opens the actual newest slot with wb, then that write fails.

Impact: A failed save can turn an already durable latest setting into an older setting on reboot. The dirty RAM retry is retained, but cannot protect against a reset during the failure interval.

Recommended change: Return separate missing/corrupt/I/O statuses. Abort writes on ambiguous read failures. Publish a new checked generation through a staged file or otherwise preserve both existing valid generations until verification; retain SD lock/lease, fsync/readback and dirty retry.

Verification/limits: Positive isolated regression includes actual prefs_disk.c and injects one read EIO plus one failed fwrite; expected current payload newest is replaced by older. checks/storage/prefs_read_error/result.log. The last device flash also observed the newest slot becoming unreadable and older racket preference returning; that observation alone does not prove this exact failure chain.

Source evidence:

  - nos/prefs_disk.c:33 — read_slot collapses absence, corrupt payload, and I/O errors into false.
  - nos/prefs_disk.c:44 — newest chooses any other readable checkpoint.
  - nos/prefs_disk.c:68 — Target selection assumes failed read means safe overwrite.
  - nos/prefs_disk.c:77 — wb truncates that slot before write/flush verification.

STOR-002 | P1 | Journal append fallback can truncate existing sports and sensor history
=======================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: storage_services

Trigger: fopen(existing_path,"r+b") fails with a transient error other than ENOENT; a subsequent fopen(path,"w+b") succeeds.

Impact: All prior records in that hourly/daily file are lost and status.available is reported true. Historical low-DMA errors motivate testing this path; they do not prove this exact truncate sequence occurred on the watch.

Recommended change: Create only on a confirmed ENOENT; preserve and retry on EIO/ENOMEM/EMFILE. Retain update-mode append at the validated tail and explicit torn-tail recovery; a plain a+b substitution would defeat seek-based recovery. Log operation, errno, path ID and retained queue count.

Verification/limits: Positive regression executes the extracted current drain body with a transient r+b EIO and verifies the existing record disappears while status.available stays true. checks/storage/journal_open_error/result.log.

Source evidence:

  - nos/sports.c:205 — Creation fallback is unconditional after update-open failure.
  - nos/sports.c:220 — Successful new write advances queue and reports success despite loss of the older file.

STOR-003 | P1 | Recording auto-resume is silently suppressed by temporary checkpoint read failures
================================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: storage_services

Trigger: At boot, both intent checkpoint fopen calls fail transiently while a valid active intent remains on the card.

Impact: The loader permanently marks intent loaded, sets inactive/uncorrupted, and reports no pending work. The crash-resume guarantee is lost without a visible checkpoint error for that boot; an explicit user start or later healthy reboot can recover.

Recommended change: Keep the resume state unknown and retry with backoff after read/I/O errors; only settle inactive after confirmed absence or the newest proven inactive generation, with no unreadable slot that might be newer. Expose loading/unknown/corrupt/active states and log which slot/operation failed.

Verification/limits: Positive regression executes the actual intent_load body with two read EIOs; active checked record remains on disk but imu_worker_pending returns false. checks/storage/imu_intent_read_error/result.log.

Source evidence:

  - nos/imu_rec.c:54 — intent_load skips every failed fopen without distinguishing ENOENT from I/O failure.
  - nos/imu_rec.c:66 — Loaded is set even when no slot could be read.
  - nos/imu_rec.c:351 — Worker retries load only while not intent_loaded.
  - nos/imu_rec.c:385 — No loaded active/dirty state means no pending resume.

STOR-004 | P1 | Failed upload publication can delete the original file
======================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: storage_services

Trigger: An upload replaces an existing path, closes its .part file, removes the original, then rename fails.

Impact: Both original and new data are deleted; an error response does not restore the original. This can affect recordings, preferences, textures, or any user path accepted by the transfer protocol.

Recommended change: Use an explicit backup/publication protocol supported by FATFS: preserve original until new file is durable and rename succeeds, recover interrupted transactions at mount, and require an opt-in replacement mode for critical settings/active recordings.

Verification/limits: Positive isolated regression executes the current do_put body with a rename EIO and verifies neither original nor staged file survives. No real device file was touched. checks/storage/upload_rename_error/result.log.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/sd_xfer.c:71 — do_put stages in a .part file.
  - firmware/main/sd_xfer.c:96 — Original is removed before publication succeeds.
  - firmware/main/sd_xfer.c:97 — Failed rename deletes .part too.

STOR-006 | P1 | Historical prolonged runtime exhausted SD sector DMA allocation headroom
========================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: storage_services

Trigger: On the previously running image, background operation eventually reached repeated 512-byte MALLOC_CAP_DMA allocation failures during SD I/O.

Impact: Settings checkpointing, sensor history and diagnostic persistence failed together. The first flash was blocked safely; resetting recovered storage, but the runtime condition has no durable fix.

Recommended change: Give SD a guaranteed bounded DMA staging resource using the installed SDK host capability or a dedicated owner; include internal-DMA headroom in admission for radio/TLS/display/recording work. Measure the failing allocation window, then run an unplugged long-session soak with settings, diagnostics and recording concurrently.

Verification/limits: Observed on OLD pre-blur running firmware: build/continuous-trail-flash/{diagnose-checkpoint.log,recover.log,recovery-boot.log,flash.log}. SDK local esp_heap_caps.h identifies 0x8 as DMA; sdmmc_cmd.c allocate_dma_buf uses this capability. A later heap snapshot with free bytes does not disprove earlier transient allocation exhaustion. Root cause allocation owner/timing remains unresolved.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/board.c:318 — SD host uses default allocation behavior; no reserved SD bounce-buffer owner is configured.
  - firmware/main/nos_heap_diag.c:50 — Failure hook captures final allocator failures.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:1280 — Display DMA buffers are reserved at startup; SD/radio resource admission is not coordinated here.

APP-001 | P2 | A GPS gap over 500 metres permanently freezes the track anchor
=============================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Record two points, lose GPS while moving over 500 m from the last accepted point, then reacquire and continue away from it.

Impact: Recording remains active, but every subsequent position is rejected and both saved route and accumulated distance omit the remaining journey until the user returns within 500 m of the old point.

Recommended change: Track the last observation and fix-gap age separately from the last stored point. On a plausible reacquisition after a gap, begin a new GPX segment/reanchor without counting the unobserved gap; preserve a bounded speed/quality outlier filter.

Verification/limits: check: build/full-os-audit-20261021/checks/content/track_reacquisition.log
result: Actual svc_track.c: after 200 later valid fixes, point count stayed 2, distance stayed 11.12 m, active stayed 1.

Source evidence:

  - nos/svc_track.c:51 — Only GPS valid and allocated point storage are required; no recovery state is maintained.
  - nos/svc_track.c:53 — Distance is always measured from the last accepted point.
  - nos/svc_track.c:55 — d > 500 returns before updating the anchor; repeating normal fixes cannot recover.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_gps.c:260 — The HAL correctly invalidates stale fixes, so a normal outage can reach the service recovery path.

APP-002 | P2 | Navigate removes the only Stop control after prolonged GPS loss
==============================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Start recording and lose the fix for at least 30 seconds, or reopen an active recording before a fresh fix arrives.

Impact: The track and GPS continue running, but the NOW page shows the sky without Stop. The user must regain GPS to stop/save through the UI; no other production caller of svc_track_stop exists.

Recommended change: Make recording state determine Stop availability independently of fix state. Keep a Stop/save action on the searching and module-absent pages and on reopening an active session.

  - nos/apps/out_nav.c:470 — The sole production call to svc_track_stop is this screen's stop_track().

APP-003 | P2 | Old BACK/TRACK guidance regains live brightness after 30 seconds without GPS
===========================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: content_apps

Trigger: While a retained track and last fix exist, remain without a new fix beyond the 30-second NOW-page stale interval, then view BACK TO START or TRACK.

Impact: The old location, arrow, and start distance continue to be used but their stale dimming fades back to the live appearance. BACK TO START can continue saying YOU ARE AT THE START based on the old fix without a current-fix warning.

Recommended change: Separate fix validity/age from the NOW-page retention timeout. Keep stale styling and a clear age/no-fix state on every navigation page, and gate arrival confirmation on a current qualified fix.

APP-004 | P2 | A failed track save cannot be retried before starting discards the session
=========================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Stop a track with at least two points while the SD card is absent or a file write/open fails; restore the card and try to save that track.

Impact: svc_track_stop turns active off before the write and later calls return -1. Navigate shows a short NO SD CARD summary and then offers Start, whose implementation discards the retained points. No pending-save/retry UI or durable session recovery exists.

Recommended change: Retain an explicit unsaved session after stop and allow retry/export until successful or explicitly discarded. Save via a worker with mount lease, shared lock, checked flush/fsync/close, and atomic publication; root's storage audit owns the common SD lifecycle findings.

Verification/limits: method: Cross-checked full production callers and all return paths; no card/device fault injection performed.
related scope: Shared SD transaction/lease findings are owned by the root storage review.

Source evidence:

  - nos/svc_track.c:132 — Stopping changes T.active=0 before write_gpx() returns a result.
  - nos/svc_track.c:135 — A second stop returns -1 when inactive, preventing a save retry.
  - nos/svc_track.c:72 — Starting resets both point count and distance.
  - nos/apps/out_nav.c:471 — All save failures are reduced to saved_state=2, including write errors unrelated to card absence.
  - nos/apps/out_nav.c:619 — The summary expires into Start rather than Retry save.

APP-005 | P2 | Recorder scans only the first 24 files before choosing displayed memos

Impact: Enumeration stops at 24 before sorting. With the usual creation/name order, newer recordings are absent from the app even after reopening, while save confirmation still succeeds. A directory with 25 fixture memos reproduced omission of the newest file.

APP-007 | P2 | Recorder confirms SAVED before finalization and cannot distinguish disk failure
==============================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Stop a memo manually, or let the recorder stop itself because the card fills or an I/O error occurs.

Impact: hal_rec_stop only requests an asynchronous stop, but the app clears s_rec_open, reloads/stats the still-open file, and immediately announces SAVED/success. Final buffered samples, WAV length header, and fclose may not be complete; real disk errors follow the same success path. Displayed duration can be derived from an incomplete file.

Recommended change: Expose recorder states RECORDING, FINALIZING, SAVED, and ERROR with a completion result and written/dropped sample counts. Add the file to the list and confirm only after checked finalization; surface recoverable/truncated recordings accurately.

Verification/limits: check: build/full-os-audit-20261021/checks/content/memo_finalize_pending.log
result: Actual app stop plus extracted actual HAL stop/active functions: toast=SAVED 0:01 while audio_recording=1 and stop_request=1.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/ext/ext_audio.c:517 — hal_rec_stop sets rec_stop_req and returns without finalization.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_audio.c:524 — hal_rec_active becomes false as soon as the request is set.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_audio.c:371 — Write error sets rec_err in the asynchronous service.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_audio.c:379 — Header rewrite and fclose occur later in audio_service.
  - nos/apps/con_memo.c:355 — svc_memo_stop immediately runs rec_finish, success feedback, and saved toast.
  - nos/apps/con_memo.c:584 — A self-stop from card-full/error also uses rec_stop's success path.

APP-008 | P2 | A covering screen stops compass calibration without resuming it
==============================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: observed | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Briefly cover the Compass during an unfinished calibration with a call, timer/alarm, or Find Watch alert, then reveal it while magnetometer samples remain available.

Impact: leave always finishes/stops HAL calibration, but reveal enter returns early and keeps M_CAL. With no previous valid calibration, progress stays at zero and the figure-eight UI cannot complete. With an old calibration, the preserved UI may instead accept the old completed status as the new attempt's result.

Recommended change: Preserve an in-progress calibration on a cover, or deliberately cancel it and move to a resumable prompt. Reconcile HAL calibration state on reveal and distinguish cancelled/failed/successful completion.

Verification/limits: check: build/full-os-audit-20261021/checks/content/compass_interruption.log
result: Actual Compass leave/reveal: mode=M_CAL, calibrating=0.

  - nos/nos.c:183 — Pushing another screen invokes the covered screen's leave callback.
  - nos/apps/out_compass.c:203 — Compass leave calls hal_mag_calibrate(0) whenever M_CAL is displayed, even on cover.
  - nos/apps/out_compass.c:187 — Reveal enter returns without restarting or reconciling calibration.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_mag.c:242 — Finish sets calibrating=0, and insufficient coverage keeps the previous calibration.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_mag.c:182 — Stopped calibration reports 0 for an uncalibrated device or 1 for an old valid calibration.

APP-009 | P2 | Compass Level claims FLAT when the IMU is unavailable
====================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Use a present/calibrated magnetometer but fail/remove the base IMU, then long-press into Level.

Impact: A failed hal_imu_read leaves the default acceleration (0,0,1), producing a centered bubble, zero tilt, and FLAT despite no measurement. The optional-sensor contract is not reflected in this internal screen.

Recommended change: Track IMU validity and render a sensor unavailable/stale state. Only show tilt or FLAT after a qualified sample; retain the magnetometer's separate availability state.

Verification/limits: method: Examined the actual failed-read branch and Level input/draw lifecycle. Hardware removal/failure was not exercised.

  - nos/apps/out_compass.c:298 — The resulting center bubble qualifies as level_ok.

APP-010 | P2 | Cellular reports SENT when an SMS was merely queued
==================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Tap any supported quick reply while the modem accepts the send request, then let registration, SIM, prompt, or network sending fail.

Impact: The reader immediately shows SENT and success feedback, then closes after 1.3 s. Actual modem completion can take up to 60 s and may fail; there is no app completion/error path for that request.

Recommended change: Return a send request ID and expose queued/sending/sent/failed status. Keep the result pending until modem acceptance, and permit retry on the actual failure without falsely confirming delivery.

Verification/limits: check: build/full-os-audit-20261021/checks/content/sms_queued_success.log
