  - nos/apps/con_cell.c:549 — Queue acceptance becomes R.result=1 immediately.
  - nos/apps/con_cell.c:598 — The successful reader auto-pops after 1.3 s.
  - nos/apps/con_cell.c:613 — R.result>0 is rendered as SENT.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:320 — Prompt/body failures increment sent_fail asynchronously.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:454 — Queueing returns success before the AT send completes.

APP-011 | P2 | Read SMS messages become unread again on the next inbox poll
===========================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Open an unread SMS, return to Cellular status so its getter keeps LTE active, and let the next periodic or incoming-message-triggered CMGL refresh complete; then reopen the inbox.

Impact: The app and HAL clear unread only in RAM. The mode-1 CMGL command deliberately preserves SIM read state, and no CMGR or other read-state operation is sent, so the next refresh restores REC UNREAD and its badge.

Recommended change: Keep the SIM's stable message index and issue the supported modem read-state update, or persist a local read overlay keyed to a stable message identity. Reconcile that overlay on every refresh.

Verification/limits: method: Traced reader -> HAL mark-read -> CMGL publication and searched all modem commands; no physical modem test.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/con_cell.c:558 — Opening a message clears app cache and invokes hal_lte_sms_mark_read.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:428 — The HAL mark-read implementation only edits L->sms[i].unread and the count.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:216 — Every new scan trusts the modem's REC UNREAD flag.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:350 — Inbox refreshes occur every 60 s and on dirty notification.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:352 — CMGL with mode 1 lists without marking read; there is no production CMGR operation in this driver.

APP-012 | P2 | An inbox refresh can make opening one SMS mark another SMS read
==============================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Leave the app inbox open and receive a newer SMS while the modem is still active (for example within the eight-second lease after inbox entry), so an incoming-triggered driver scan replaces/reorders its cached list; then open a row from the app's older snapshot.

Impact: The app's displayed message remains from the old s_sms array, but mark_read(s_open) applies the old row index to the new driver order. The wrong message and unread count are changed.

Recommended change: Include stable modem message IDs and snapshot generation in hal_sms. Resolve read/update operations by ID rather than by a transient row position, and refresh inbox counts as its generation changes.

Verification/limits: method: Application snapshot and driver mutation ordering checked; the UI task and driver task can reach the sequence without unsynchronized array access.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/con_cell.c:439 — The app takes its SMS snapshot on inbox_enter.
  - nos/apps/con_cell.c:450 — Inbox tick does not refresh or bind rows to a stable message identifier.
  - nos/apps/con_cell.c:559 — Reader marks the message by s_open, a row index.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:242 — Background publish sorts newest-first and replaces the driver list.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:432 — Mark-read uses the same integer as the current driver's index.

APP-013 | P2 | World Clock infers the wrong day and relative offset in overlapping extreme timezones
================================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Use a valid UTC epoch and a configured positive extreme timezone, e.g. UTC+13 at 2026-10-21 16:00 UTC (local Thursday 05:00), and view London.

Impact: Time-of-day subtraction infers UTC-11 instead of UTC+13. London displays the correct clock hour modulo 24 but the wrong weekday/day relationship and a relative offset differing by 24 h. Similar ambiguity exists for overlapping positive/negative offset ranges.

Recommended change: Use the authoritative configured/effective timezone offset or calculate local-civil-epoch minus UTC using the full date. Add tests around UTC-12, UTC+12/+13/+14 and date-line rollover.

Verification/limits: check: build/full-os-audit-20261021/checks/content/worldclock_offset.log
result: Configured +780 min inferred -660; London Thursday/same day/+720 instead of Wednesday/previous day/-720.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/time_worldclock.c:133 — local_offset_min compares only local and UTC time-of-day and discards calendar day.
  - nos/apps/time_worldclock.c:141 — The accepted range spans 26 hours and cannot uniquely unwrap that difference.
  - nos/apps/time_worldclock.c:151 — The inferred offset determines city day rollover and relative offset.
  - nos/apps/time_worldclock.c:157 — Weekday is based on that erroneous rollover.

APP-014 | P2 | Weather retains invalidated and indefinitely stale data as current
=================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Receive weather, then stay offline long enough for it to become stale, or change the configured weather city while the next fetch cannot complete.

Impact: NOS ignores HAL stale/age metadata. If HAL later invalidates its cache on a city change, wx_from_hal simply returns and preserves the prior valid feed. App/widget treat any valid bit as WS_OK and show old weather with no age/source warning.

Recommended change: Propagate source, observed age, fetching/error status, and location generation through svc_weather_t. Invalidate on explicit location change, keep stale content visibly dated, and never label old-city data as a successful new-city feed.

Verification/limits: check: build/full-os-audit-20261021/checks/content/weather_invalidated_cache.log
result: HAL valid=0/New city leaves NOS valid=1/Old city; a two-day stale HAL snapshot was accepted without qualification.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/weather_service.c:37 — Changing location explicitly invalidates the HAL cache.
  - firmware/main/weather_service.c:66 — The HAL exposes stale after one hour.
  - nos/svc.c:357 — An invalid HAL cache leaves the existing NOS weather unchanged.
  - nos/svc.c:359 — A valid HAL cache is copied without stale/age metadata.
  - nos/svc.c:375 — The phone freshness window only selects a source; it does not expire old NOS data when HAL has none.
  - nos/apps/out_weather.c:358 — Any w->valid is immediately WS_OK irrespective of observation time or connection state.

APP-015 | P2 | Weather forecast values are relabelled with the watch's current hours and days
=============================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Keep a forecast through an hour/day boundary, or use a configured weather location with a different timezone from the watch.

Impact: HAL forecast entries carry UTC timestamps and the location's UTC offset, but the service drops them. The app labels unchanged cached values using current watch hour+1 and weekday+i; an old/yesterday or other-location entry is presented as a new future hour/TODAY.

Recommended change: Preserve entry epochs and the forecast location timezone. Select entries against the current trusted epoch, render their actual local times/dates, and drop expired entries rather than moving their labels.

Verification/limits: method: Traced actual HAL schema, service conversion, and app label creation; no live HTTP request was needed.

Source evidence:

  - core/weather.h:6 — weather_hour and weather_day carry epoch; hal_weather also carries utc_offset_seconds.
  - nos/svc.c:341 — wx_merge_forecast copies only values/conditions and discards per-entry timestamps.
  - nos/apps/out_weather.c:545 — Display hour/weekday always come from the current watch local datetime.
  - nos/apps/out_weather.c:621 — Hourly labels are generated from current hour + 1 + row, regardless of forecast epoch.
  - nos/apps/out_weather.c:655 — Daily row zero is always TODAY, and later labels use current watch weekday.

APP-016 | P2 | An ended alert covered by another alert can remain orphaned in the screen stack
==============================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: observed | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Let an alarm or timer alert expire/end while another ring screen covers it; then dismiss the covering ring.

Impact: ring_screen clears pushed merely because the alert is not on top. It does not remove the covered stack entry. Later it no longer owns cleanup; alarm/timer reveal initialization does not reject their ended service state, so a stale alert remains visible/awake and may require explicit user dismissal. Find Watch has a similar was_active-on-reveal limitation.

Recommended change: Retain alert ownership while covered and reconcile/remove ended entries on reveal, or give every ring screen a service generation plus an ended-state close path. Verify simultaneous and nested alarms, timers, calls, and Find Watch transitions.

Verification/limits: check: build/full-os-audit-20261021/checks/content/covered_alert.log
result: Actual ring_screen() with a narrow stack seam: want=0, pushed=0, but ALARM remains the top entry after the cover is popped.

Source evidence:

  - nos/svc.c:605 — The ended-screen branch begins cleanup tracking.
  - nos/svc.c:608 — If it is covered and no transition runs, pushed/ending are cleared without removing the stack entry.
  - nos/apps/time_alarm.c:873 — Alarm ring enter builds a default ALARM even when svc_alarm_ringing() is -1.
  - nos/apps/time_alarm.c:903 — Its tick has no service-state ended guard.
  - nos/apps/time_timer.c:892 — Timer ring tick likewise does not dismiss when the service timer is inactive.
  - nos/apps/con_find.c:414 — Find Watch initializes was_active from the already-ended state, preventing its false-after-true auto-close check on reveal.

APP-017 | P2 | Music air gestures are not delivered from hardware and stop being requested
==========================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Open Music on a watch with the APDS gesture sensor and attempt the implemented up/down/left/right controls.

Impact: Music enables the gesture engine once and handles IN_AIR, but production code never drains hal_light_gesture into nos_air. The only hardware drain is the Modules diagnostic. The gesture driver also disables the engine after eight idle seconds because Music never refreshes/drains it. Simulator air injection can conceal the missing device path.

Recommended change: Have one active-consumer-aware hardware input pump drain APDS gestures into nos_air, refreshing demand only where gestures are used. Release demand on leave and test the production HAL path beyond eight seconds.

Verification/limits: method: Whole-repo call-site search and driver idle/service lifecycle inspection. Physical gesture recognition remains an unverified hardware capability.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/con_music.c:358 — Music requests gestures only on enter.
  - nos/apps/con_music.c:763 — Control handlers require engine IN_AIR events.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_light.c:173 — The request auto-expires after NOS_EXT_IDLE_MS without refresh.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_light.c:235 — Queue drain is also the gesture keepalive.
  - nos/nos.c:924 — nos_air is the event injection entry, but whole-repo production call-site search found no hardware caller.
  - nos/apps/set_modules.c:950 — The diagnostic drains gesture data locally without forwarding it to Music.

APP-021 | P2 | Phone alarm synchronization can silently overwrite a different alarm being edited
================================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Open an existing watch alarm editor, then receive a Gadgetbridge alarm-list replacement containing a different alarm at that same index while the editor remains open.

Impact: The editor retains only the numeric index and its old drum values. If the new table still has that index, ed_valid passes; the next edit tick writes the old wheel time into the replacement alarm and leaving persists it. No generation/identity check detects the replacement.

Recommended change: Give alarm tables a generation and alarms stable identities. On remote replacement, rebind/reload the editor or close it with a visible update message; never apply a stale draft to a new alarm solely because its row number matches.

Verification/limits: method: Traced phone_tick before app tick, full-table replacement, editor validity, drum update, and leave persistence. No paired phone used.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/time_alarm.c:483 — The editor binds to s_edit_idx only.
  - nos/apps/time_alarm.c:595 — ed_valid checks index bounds, not table generation or alarm identity.
  - nos/apps/time_alarm.c:660 — The wheels are initialized from the selected alarm only when entering.
  - nos/apps/time_alarm.c:700 — Each tick copies the retained wheel time into whichever alarm now occupies that index.
  - nos/svc.c:1008 — Gadgetbridge synchronization clears and replaces the entire alarm table in the service tick.
  - nos/apps/time_alarm.c:670 — Leaving saves the editor's mutations.

APP-022 | P2 | The LTE radio loses its lease while the SMS inbox or reader is still open
========================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Enter the SMS inbox or reader with radio enabled and remain there for more than NOS_EXT_IDLE_MS (8 s), without queuing a reply or another LTE getter being called.

Impact: The driver's request expires and it powers the modem off even though the user is actively using a Cellular screen. New messages/registration updates stop until returning to the status screen or another action wakes the modem; app inbox contents and enabled preference do not reveal that power transition.

Recommended change: Keep an explicit LTE lease while any foreground Cellular status/inbox/reader screen requires live connectivity, and release it on leave. Show actual radio state if cached-only/offline browsing is intentional.

Verification/limits: method: Traced all LTE getter/list call sites, screen-stack ticking semantics and driver want_on/power-off guards. A pending send is an inspected exception and renews demand.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/con_cell.c:441 — Inbox entry refreshes the SMS list and renews the radio lease only once.
  - nos/apps/con_cell.c:450 — Inbox tick only advances UI/pending navigation and never refreshes LTE demand.
  - nos/apps/con_cell.c:582 — Reader tick also has no LTE demand/getter keepalive.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:259 — enabled is cleared after last_get expires when no send is pending.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:334 — The service requests modem power-off when demand expires.
  - nos/hal_ext.h:35 — NOS_EXT_IDLE_MS is eight seconds.

DEV-002 | P2 | Settings GPS auto changes a preference with no device-side consumer
==================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Toggle Settings > GPS auto on an equipped watch and compare with the Control Center GPS tile, or reboot after saving the setting.

Impact: The Settings row and Control Center display the same bit, but only the tile calls hal_gps_enable. No active ESP firmware consumer applies gps_auto on boot or preference change; its other reader is inside the simulator-only Sports path. The row cannot reliably start/stop the configured GPS demand and the persisted display can disagree with the radio demand. This does not imply GPS getters stop requesting their own leases.

Recommended change: Define auto versus explicit GPS demand and apply the preference through one shared routine from both controls and boot. Expose actual demand, lease owner and powered state separately in Modules.

Verification/limits: Repository-wide gps_auto caller search, active compile manifest and driver enable/get paths inspected; no physical receiver test.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/set_settings.c:1732 — Generic toggle writes preference, animation and prefs_save only.
  - nos/apps/sys_control.c:231 — Control Center changes same preference and calls hal_gps_enable(on).
  - nos/sports.c:337 — Only additional gps_auto reader342 is under #ifndef ESP_PLATFORM.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_gps.c:290 — Driver enable and getter lease state are independent from nos_prefs.

DEV-003 | P2 | Wrist selection and learned wake position use independent wrist values
=====================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Select the right wrist in current Settings, then use Learn reading angle or expect an existing per-wrist reading pose to switch.

Impact: Settings changes nos_prefs.wrist_left, while wake_service chooses and learns s_cfg.view[s_cfg.wrist]. That independent stored wrist defaults left and current NOS never calls wake_service_set("wrist",...). A right-wrist selection therefore does not select the right wake pose; learning overwrites the currently selected wake_cfg hand. A legacy UI has the setter but is not in the active NOS build.

Recommended change: Unify the wrist authority or explicitly migrate/synchronize the wake wrist when Settings changes. Translate left/right enum meanings carefully. Preserve the two learned poses and expose which one is active in the learning result.

Verification/limits: All wake_service_set callers and NOS serial wake handler inspected. Incorrect hand selection is proven; physical gesture sensitivity impact is unmeasured.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/set_settings.c:1703 — flip(I_WRIST) changes only nos_prefs.wrist_left and saves.
  - nos/apps/set_settings.c:1771 — Learn reading angle directly invokes wake_service_learn.
  - core/wake_service.c:45 — Configuration selects view by s_cfg.wrist; default is0 at55.
  - core/wake_service.c:73 — Independent wrist changes only through this setter.
  - core/wake_service.c:117 — Learning writes next.view[next.wrist].
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:1337 — NOS initializes wake from raise_wake only.
  - core/apps/app_settings.c:497 — The legacy Settings UI does call the setter, but it is inactive for current1.43-B NOS.

Impact: nos_push invokes the covered screen leave callback. keyboard_leave unconditionally clears N.pass, and the keyboard has no enter callback to restore a draft. The same password field is revealed empty and connection attempts can fail after an unrelated interruption.

Recommended change: Preserve the in-memory draft on a temporary cover; scrub it when the user actually leaves, connects or cancels. Add a cover/reveal test with no secret text in logs.

DEV-006 | P2 | Diagnostics mistakes legitimate constant light readings for a frozen sensor
==========================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: observed | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Run Diagnostics with a working light sensor held in darkness and stable proximity, producing fresh valid0-lux/0-proximity readings for4 seconds.

Impact: Each UI poll is counted as a read and equality of extrema is treated as proof of a stopped stream. A steady functioning sensor fails READING FROZEN. No sample sequence or age establishes acquisition liveness; the UI does not request a stimulus before that automatic verdict. Similar equality-based mic/mag checks warrant explicit stimulus semantics.

Recommended change: Use sample timestamps or acquisition sequence numbers for liveness. If response-to-motion/light is tested, prompt for a controlled change and distinguish inconclusive from failed.

Verification/limits: diag_constant_light.log:160 deliberately fresh valid constant samples were classified ST_FAIL/READING FROZEN by the actual test routine.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/set_diag.c:341 — Light check polls the cached getter and accumulates values.
  - nos/apps/set_diag.c:350 — lt_n counts getter polls, not unique acquisitions.
  - nos/apps/set_diag.c:357 — At10+ identical lux/proximity polls it fails READING FROZEN.
  - nos/apps/set_diag.c:377 — Compass has a related no-change predicate; hardware motion is not inferred here.

DEV-007 | P2 | Simulator store replacement frees the old value before a fallible allocation
===========================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Save an existing simulated key, then force allocation failure on replacement and subsequently load the same-sized key.

Impact: hal_store_save_checked returns failure after destroying the prior RAM value; size remains the prior positive value and hal_store_load can memcpy from NULL. Simulator failure behavior violates the checked-store contract and cannot validate that a failed preference save preserves the last good state. Interactive file persistence also uses direct wb replacement rather than the real SD checkpoints.

Recommended change: Allocate a replacement first, commit only after success, and leave old state intact on failure. Provide a firmware-compatible checkpoint fixture or clearly separate generic simulator storage from durability tests.

Verification/limits: sim_store_allocation.log:old42 saved, forced allocation failure leaves slotdataNULL,size4. The crash-causing NULL copy was inferred, not executed.

Source evidence:

  - sim/nos_hal_sim.c:213 — Old data is freed before malloc; failure215 returns withNULL and old size.
  - sim/nos_hal_sim.c:232 — Load checks size only, then memcpy234 from the possiblyNULL value.
  - sim/nos_hal_sim.c:222 — Interactive persistence truncates the single mirror directly, unlike firmware paired checksummed checkpoints.

DEV-008 | P2 | Default simulator builds can succeed with old code after a source compile failure
================================================================================================

Category: dev_qol | confidence: code_proven | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Edit a source with an existing cached object so it fails to compile; run the documented default sim/nos_build.ps1 without -Strict, then use that binary for a regression capture.

Impact: The build deliberately links the last good object, prints a warning, and can exit successfully. The resulting executable mixes source revisions but has no per-object provenance/degraded marker for downstream test/capture tools. A named -Out binary still shares the same object cache. This is an intentional interactive convenience with an insufficient verification boundary.

Recommended change: Make verification/package/test profiles require -Strict and carry a source/flags/compiler/object manifest. Keep an explicit interactive fallback switch with a visible degraded label that capture/report tools reject.

Verification/limits: Build control flow and downstream invocation profiles inspected; no shared simulator build performed.

Source evidence:

  - sim/nos_build.ps1:93 — Compile failures throw only for -Strict or missing old object.
  - sim/nos_build.ps1:99 — Default explicitly warns then links last-good objects.
  - sim/nos_build.ps1:48 — All named outputs share build/nos_obj or nos_obj_nocost.
  - docs/NOS_SIM.md:34 — Documentation explains fallback; therefore this is not described as a hidden behavior.

DEV-009 | P2 | Simulator -Verify compares against stale HEAD and does not gate native command failures
================================================================================================

Category: dev_qol | confidence: code_proven | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Run -Verify in this recovered dirty workspace, or repeat verification after a compiler/executable fails when old outputs remain in its fixed temporary directory.

Impact: The reference is git HEAD, which predates recovered rendering changes, rather than the same current source with hooks disabled. GCC, git show, executables and final --accuracy have no explicit exit-code gates in the Verify block; existing equal a.bin/b.bin can survive a failed run and produce a misleading pass. Fixed nos_cost_verify and shared object caches also permit interference between runs.

Recommended change: Compare the same snapshotted current sources compiled with/without hooks; use a GUID temp directory, remove/avoid preexisting outputs, require successful exit codes and exact output sizes, and carry both source hashes into the verification report.

Verification/limits: Static control-flow proof. Stale-output false-pass scenario was not executed; the pixel-difference assertion itself is not claimed to be wrong.

Source evidence:

  - sim/nos_build.ps1:119 — Fixed TEMP/nos_cost_verify directory is reused.
  - sim/nos_build.ps1:122 — Reference gfx/font/sprite are fetched from HEAD.
  - sim/nos_build.ps1:125 — Both compiler calls and executable invocations proceed to hash comparison without LASTEXITCODE checks.
  - sim/nos_build.ps1:129 — Only hashes of the named output files gate success; freshness is not checked.
  - sim/nos_build.ps1:132 — --accuracy exit code is not checked before later success paths.
  - AGENTS.md:3 — Workspace recovery instructions explicitly state that HEAD predates the intended source.

DEV-010 | P2 | Packaged flashing bypasses the preference checkpoint used by the supported updater
================================================================================================

Category: dev_qol | confidence: code_proven | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Use the generated dist/v1 README and flash.ps1 to update the current1.43-B watch while runtime preferences remain dirty or SD persistence is failing.

Impact: The package correctly uses separate segments and warns against flashing the merged image over NVS, but flash-package.ps1 immediately resets/writes through esptool. It never sends prefs flush or requires stored=1/match=1, so recent authoritative SD preferences can be lost across reboot. It also lacks the checked range validation used by flash_watch. NVS preservation alone does not establish SD preference durability.

Recommended change: Package a common checked flasher with segment manifest validation and explicit SD checkpoint acknowledgment. Preserve the existing merged-image warning; do not substitute another checkout. Keep C143 legacy variant flashing documented separately because it uses a different runtime.

Verification/limits: Actual flash_watch range helper accepted safe synthetic ranges and rejected9000/8f80 overlaps. No serial checkpoint, esptool write or reset executed.

  - tools/package.ps1:15 — Copies flash-package.ps1 into the distributed flash.ps1 and README directs users to it.

  - tools/flash_watch.py:8 — Supported updater first waits for a checked NOSPREFS flush acknowledgment.
  - tools/flash_watch.py:22 — Supported updater validates segment/NVS overlap before reset.
  - AGENTS.md:8 — Workspace requires the checked preference-preserving update route.

DEV-011 | P2 | Release packages omit the source identity and symbols needed to reproduce a device report
================================================================================================

Category: dev_qol | confidence: code_proven | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Archive a dist/v1 package, flash it, continue editing this dirty recovered workspace, then investigate a crash or performance capture weeks later.

Impact: The package records binary hashes and honest hardware-evidence matches, but omits watchfw.elf/map, sdkconfig/dependency lock, current source manifest/archive and compiler identity. About only shows hardcoded1.0.0 plus compile date; serial info can report the ELF hash but the matching ELF is not bundled. Emit its short ID in About and trace boot metadata. Add required Solar/Orrery/Developer/home pager/sports feature checks and app-slot headroom reporting from existing build artifacts before packaging.

Verification/limits: Package outputs, About fields and serial/report identity contracts inspected. No firmware build or symbolication performed; canonical OBS-001 covers missing crash PC/backtrace collection.

Source evidence:

  - tools/package.ps1:7 — Package copies only bootloader, partition table and app binaries.
  - tools/package.ps1:43 — Manifest hashes the four binary outputs; fields60-66 lack ELF/source/config identity.
  - nos/apps/set_about.c:113 — Displayed version uses NOS_VERSION and __DATE__.
  - nos/nos.h:25 — NOS_VERSION is hardcoded1.0.0.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:1189 — Existing NOSBUILD reports app_elf_sha256, providing a useful join key.
  - tools/nos_perf_report.py:114 — Performance reporter already requires source_revision/tree_sha256/firmware_elf_sha256 identity.

DEV-015 | P2 | Set time claims success when the unset calendar makes the HAL reject the update
==============================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Boot with an unset/untrusted RTC and no network/phone clock, open Settings > Set time, choose an hour/minute and tap SET.

Impact: board_restore_clock explicitly sets epoch0, yielding year1970. The UI preserves that date and edits only hour/minute. Firmware hal_set_datetime rejects years outside2000..2099, but its void API gives no result and the UI unconditionally shows TIME SET/success and exits. The time remains unset, leaving alarms/day grouping unreliable until an external full-date sync. The missing date entry is a feature limitation; the false success is a reachable bug.

Recommended change: Return a checked clock-set result; show failure/DATE NOT SET and keep the editor open when the calendar is invalid. Provide a full-date setup or clearly direct users to phone/Wi-Fi setup. Extend simulation to enforce the firmware validator.

Verification/limits: manual_time_unset_date.log:actual time_input plus production board validator rejects1970 while app pops and emits TIME SET. HAL rejection is modeled using its inspected validation rule; no clock/device changed.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/board.c:507 — Untrusted RTC path sets time to epoch0 instead of fabricating a date.
  - firmware/main/board_protocol.c:21 — Datetime validator rejects years below2000.
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:117 — Invalid date is logged and returns without changing clock.
  - nos/apps/set_settings.c:2268 — SET retains current date and replaces only time fields.
  - nos/apps/set_settings.c:2271 — App always success-feedbacks, toasts TIME SET and pops after void HAL call.

ENG-001 | P2 | Incoming notifications can change the target of an active swipe dismissal

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: engine

ENG-002 | P2 | Unbounded float animation time drifts and eventually stops advancing at ordinary cadence
================================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: observed | domain: engine

Trigger: Accumulate a large active animation-clock origin. At nos_t=65536 seconds,25ms advances by23.4375ms; at131072 and262144 seconds it advances by31.25ms. At524288 seconds a25ms frame advances by0.

Impact: Animations/deadlines drift well before the final freeze. At the freeze, frame count and nos_dt still advance but nos_t-based tweens, long press, toast/banner timeouts and power-sheet deadlines can stop completing. Wall uptime to encounter this depends on active/AOD/sleep frame cadence;524288 seconds is a clock value, not a claimed six-day wall-clock soak.

Recommended change: Represent monotonic animation deadlines with an integer64-bit clock and convert bounded relative intervals to float, or otherwise establish a consistent precision-preserving timestamp API. Merely changing the accumulator to double while leaving every app t0 as float does not solve relative-time precision.

Verification/limits: Current nos.c privately compiled and executed; injected five large origins and called real nos_frame_run(25). Keep these origins in a regression and verify tween, long-press and timeout completion at each.

Source evidence:

  - nos/nos.c:26 — Animation time is stored as global float.
  - nos/nos.c:2241 — nos_dt is computed from elapsed_ms, then directly accumulated into nos_t at line2242 without an epoch/precision policy.
  - nos/motion.c:30 — Authored frame count is derived from float nos_t minus float origin; tween progress uses the same difference at line327.
  - nos/nos.c:871 — Touch long-press recognition compares the affected clock against P.t_down.
  - tests/nos_engine_clock_test.c:56 — Existing clock test intentionally resets to a small origin at line58, so it does not cover uptime precision loss.
  - build/full-os-audit-20261021/checks/engine/repro.log:10 — Current-engine test observed CLOCK origin=524288 requested=.025 actual=.000000000 while dt=.025000000.

FACE-001 | P2 | MATRIX keeps the old 12/24-hour pattern until the next minute
=============================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: faces

Trigger: Display MATRIX at 13:37 with 24-hour clock enabled, change Settings 24-hour clock within the same minute, and return to HOME.

Impact: The hour continues to display 13 instead of 1 until minute/hour changes. Stale wake handling only copies the cached old pattern and does not rebuild for this preference.

Recommended change: Include hour24 or formatted HH/MM in the pattern key and rebuild both current/previous patterns when format changes.

Verification/limits: Actual-source host repro observed unchanged pattern after toggle and correct rebuild after advancing to 13:38; checks/faces/matrix-contract-repro.log.

Source evidence:

  - nos/faces/face_matrix.c:185 — Pattern rebuild key is only hour*60+minute; neither hour24 nor resulting digit strings are compared.
  - nos/faces/face_matrix.c:193 — Stale return copies now to prev without recomputing now.
  - nos/apps/set_settings.c:459 — Reachable 24-hour clock toggle.
  - nos/apps/sys_home.c:430 — Home reveal does not call face.enter to invalidate MATRIX.

FACE-002 | P2 | SPLIT temperature ignores the Imperial unit preference
======================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: faces

Trigger: Enable Imperial units with valid weather and select SPLIT.

Impact: TMP continues showing rounded Celsius with only a degree sign, while Weather shows Fahrenheit. Example 20 C remains20 instead of68, with no unit cue.

Recommended change: Use the same temperature conversion/rounding helper as Weather and include a C/F cue where practical.

Verification/limits: Source call chain and reachable Settings control verified; no runtime temperature sensor claim.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/sys_home.c:478 — Face context explicitly receives weather temp_c.
  - nos/faces/face_split.c:79 — S.temp is copied directly from temp_c.
  - nos/faces/face_split.c:135 — TMP formats S.temp without units_metric conversion.
  - nos/apps/out_weather.c:437 — Weather converts to Fahrenheit when units_metric is false.
  - nos/apps/set_settings.c:1705 — User Units control toggles units_metric.

FACE-003 | P2 | SEGMENT replaces a valid zero active-minute total with a step estimate
======================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: faces

Trigger: Accumulate steps without a qualifying >60-steps/min active minute; view SEGMENT.

Impact: The MIN value/ring invents steps/110 minutes although the service reports zero. In the focused test1100 steps/0 minutes becomes10 minutes; Activity uses the service total.

Recommended change: Use the authoritative active_min unconditionally, or add a separate explicit validity flag if estimated mode is needed.

Verification/limits: Actual-source host repro passed with service active_min=0/displayed10; checks/faces/segment-contract-repro.log.

Source evidence:

  - nos/faces/face_segment.c:244 — active_min >0 is used as availability; zero becomes steps/110.
  - nos/svc.c:400 — Authoritative active_min is the saved cadence-counted value, including zero.
  - nos/svc.c:491 — Active minutes increment only on the cadence condition.

FACE-004 | P2 | Several watch faces lose their basic clock when face/font allocation is denied
==============================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: faces

Trigger: Select MATRIX or Orrery before its mandatory state can be allocated, or select SEGMENT while a scaled-font glyph allocation remains denied.

Impact: MATRIX returns no drawing commands; Orrery returns before drawing its clock; SEGMENT draws rings/data but draw_time returns without time. The shell still draws its own overlays, so this is loss of the face clock rather than a claimed whole-panel hang.

Recommended change: Render a small allocation-free clock and an optional availability cue until required state/fonts are ready; keep the existing retry and ownership behavior.

Verification/limits: MATRIX mandatory-allocation denial observed zero commands in isolated host test. SEGMENT/Orrery variants are source-proven guard paths, not separately injected current-device failures.

Source evidence:

  - nos/faces/face_matrix.c:98 — Denied mx_state allocation exits build.
  - nos/faces/face_matrix.c:324 — draw returns immediately without fallback time.
  - nos/faces/face_segment.c:323 — No time drawn until s_font.ok.
  - nos/faces/faces.c:679 — A denied glyph leaves scaled font unfinished and retryable.
  - nos/faces/face_orrery.c:366 — Denied main state allocation returns false.
  - nos/faces/face_orrery.c:2136 — draw returns immediately when mandatory state is unavailable.

FACE-005 | P2 | A failed card photo load never retries after the SD asset becomes available
===========================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: faces

Trigger: Open a texture-backed card with absent/unreadable file or SD; let the failure complete; restore the valid asset while leaving that body open.

Impact: Failure adopts the returned buffers, advances stage and eventually removes LOADING. PHOTO renders a tinted fallback indefinitely until body reset/reopen, with no failure cue or retry.

Recommended change: Track failed stages distinctly, show PHOTO UNAVAILABLE/fallback state and retry on explicit action or bounded SD-ready recovery without violating page ownership.

Verification/limits: Actual-source host repro: missing Sun asset -> stage3/tex0; create valid1048576-byte asset -> still stage3/tex0 after200 load/poll iterations; checks/faces/card-asset-retry-repro.log.

Source evidence:

  - nos/faces/orrery_card.c:699 — Both ASSET_OK and ASSET_FAILED are adopted; only cancellation is excluded.
  - nos/faces/orrery_card.c:707 — Failure clears tex_ok but stage still increments at712.
  - nos/faces/orrery_card.c:721 — stage>=3 exits permanently.
  - nos/faces/orrery_card.c:1640 — PHOTO remains selected; LOADING only depends on stage<3.
  - nos/faces/orrery_card.c:982 — Missing texture silently substitutes ob_tint.

FACE-006 | P2 | Kepler solvers return incorrect positions for bundled high-eccentricity bodies
==============================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: faces

Trigger: Travel to dates such as JD2465445.0 (2038-01-21 12:00 UTC) for the bundled C/2025 A6 (Lemmon), e=0.9955. This lies inside the documented planet-model date range.

Impact: The8-step Newton solve can diverge and still feed sin/cos and visible positions. Actual source returns E=-1963363234.2687581 with residual1963363235.2327611rad; public and cached radius68.681648957AU differs from monotone-bisection solution28.512007850AU. Reduced residual0.1747377508rad and3D position separation40.455233130AU exclude an equivalent extra-revolution alias. A separate actual-face probe of its3-iteration belt solve at2026-10-21 (JD2461334.5), index3020/e≈0.993, differs by9.025477 projected pixels in BEYOND from a bracketed solve using the same basis. These are numerical failures, independent of external ephemeris accuracy;9px is a host source result, not a device measurement.

Recommended change: Use convergent safeguarded solvers or validate residual and fall back for both double conics and the separate float belt path; add actual high-e catalog cases across supported dates and exact/preview parity with an independent solution.

Verification/limits: Compiled actual core/ephem.c with actual catalog; independent monotone bisection comparison, reduced residual and public/cached position checks in checks/faces/ephem-residual-repro.log. Separately compiled actual face_orrery.c and compared belt_exact to a bracketed solution using the identical basis/projection in checks/faces/belt-residual-repro.log. Existing ephem_test still passes.

Source evidence:

  - core/ephem.c:58 — Initial estimate and8 unconstrained Newton iterations; no residual/convergence failure return.
  - core/ephem.c:356 — Cached elliptic conic path consumes this solver.
  - core/ephem.c:410 — Public elliptic conic path consumes the same solver.
  - core/ephem.c:378 — Preview fallback can call the same exact solver.
  - nos/faces/orrery_data.c:4041 — Actual Lemmon catalog record has e=0.9955,q=0.53,tp=2460988.04.
  - nos/faces/face_orrery.c:706 — Face evaluates this actual comet through cached/preview conic path.
  - nos/faces/face_orrery.c:558 — Separate float belt solver stops after3 Newton steps with no residual check.
  - nos/faces/orrery_data.c:3030 — Actual belt index3020 has e=0.993 and mean anomaly0.1degrees near pack epoch.

FACE-007 | P2 | Opening Events freezes a partially completed astronomy scan and row list
========================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: observed | domain: faces

  - nos/faces/orrery_events.c:110 — Events tick only advances list animation.
  - nos/nos.c:2045 — Once transition ends, only top screen ticks.

FACE-009 | P2 | Orrery time travel silently exceeds its declared ephemeris validity interval
============================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: faces

Impact: Planet/event/card calculations keep presenting ordinary precise-looking results outside the Table1 interval declared by ephem.h. This audit does not quantify the error outside that interval.

Recommended change: Clamp to a documented supported range or visibly label extrapolation and stop accuracy-sensitive event/card claims outside it.

Verification/limits: Complete time-input, momentum, jump and render callers inspected; no external accuracy assertion or extreme-date hardware test.

FACE-012 | P2 | SEGMENT cold entry synchronously completes work that background preparation normally slices
================================================================================================

Category: performance | confidence: strong_inference | domain: faces

Trigger: Select/preview SEGMENT soon after startup, before its background font/ring warm-up completes, or after warm-up was delayed by allocation pressure.

Impact: A single UI tick can finish all remaining scaled glyphs, allocate/initialize4 quadrant sprites, compute per-row ring spans and raster all180 empty segments before rendering. This stacks unbounded cold work on an interactive frame; current board deadline impact was not measured.

Recommended change: Retain bounded preparation on visible cold entry and draw a basic clock/ring fallback while it completes; qualify cold entry separately from warmed steady-state.

Verification/limits: Code path and warm/cold guards inspected; performance risk is strong inference, not a new measured30-FPS failure.

Source evidence:

  - nos/faces/face_segment.c:230 — Active tick calls cache_init then full fk_sfont_build.
  - nos/faces/faces.c:687 — Full builder loops all remaining glyphs.
  - nos/faces/face_segment.c:139 — Cold cache allocates4sprites and initializes row spans.
  - nos/faces/face_segment.c:258 — Active cold path calls cold_track(NRING*NSEG), not WARM_SEGS.
  - nos/faces/face_segment.c:304 — Background warm path normally limits glyph/track work per frame.

HEALTH-002 | P2 | Heart live display treats a cached reading as fresh and can retain invalidated values as current
================================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: The HR strap remains present and hal_hr_get keeps returning its cached struct after FIFO acquisition stops; alternatively, a locked reading is followed by on_wrist=true with bpm/SpO2 becoming unavailable or low quality.

Impact: One old acquisition can satisfy two seconds of contact and lock a current-looking BPM. Once locked, zero/unavailable BPM and SpO2 do not clear the displayed values while contact remains true. Leaving records the retained BPM with the current wall-clock time.

Recommended change: Track acquisition timestamps and age independently from attachment/contact. Count lock duration only across fresh acceptable samples; distinguish last reading from live value and clear or label invalid BPM and SpO2. Record the actual accepted acquisition time.

Verification/limits: Source-traced against the HAL cache contract and the workout recorder freshness guard at workout.c280-302. Isolated cache observation proves a getter can provide one unchanged acquisition repeatedly; the full rendered Heart state machine was not executed by this audit.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:259 — sample_ms advances only after a successful nonempty FIFO acquisition.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:303 — hal_hr_get returns sensor presence and the cached H->cur, without an age filter.
  - nos/apps/fit_heart.c:616 — Presence is taken from hal_hr_get; 624 sets fresh=1 without checking sample_ms or acquisition identity.
  - nos/apps/fit_heart.c:629 — Contact/good times accumulate UI dt from repeated cached values; 637 locks after the elapsed thresholds.
  - nos/apps/fit_heart.c:648 — Locked BPM changes only for another positive value; 651 similarly retains SpO2 when the new value is zero.
  - nos/apps/fit_heart.c:550 — Leaving records H.bpm_shown/H.spo2; svc_heart_record at svc.c571 stamps the current epoch.

HEALTH-003 | P2 | Breathe averages repeated cached HR values without age or acquisition checks
==============================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: A breathing session polls an attached strap whose last cached HR is still on_wrist/quality-valid, including an acquisition from before the session or after a FIFO stall. UI frame cadence varies.

Impact: The summary average can be based entirely on an old reading and is weighted by render-frame polling frequency rather than accepted acquisition time. It is then recorded as a new current reading.

Recommended change: Accept only new, in-session, age-bounded acquisitions; accumulate a declared time-weighted average with valid coverage and show unavailable when coverage is insufficient. Preserve sample-time provenance when recording the result.

Verification/limits: checks/health/checks.json hr_cache_observation: actual extracted hr_sample accepted100calls with identical sample_ms=1, average123.0. This reproduces the acceptance defect, not physiological accuracy.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/fit_breathe.c:167 — hr_sample checks presence, on_wrist, BPM range and quality only; 172 increments sum and count for every call.
  - nos/apps/fit_breathe.c:284 — INTRO samples HR every tick; SESSION does so at 310.
  - nos/apps/fit_breathe.c:332 — Completion averages the per-frame count, then svc_heart_record at 334 assigns a current reading timestamp.

HEALTH-004 | P2 | Heart history fabricates acquisition times from the current clock
===================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: heart_tick runs again with the same unchanged HAL history, or background samples were delayed/missing. The ring carries BPM bytes but no acquisition timestamps.

Impact: The latest nonzero reading repeatedly becomes new again; the Home widget can report JUST NOW indefinitely for an unchanged old sample. Chart/day/minimum assumptions also cannot reliably reflect actual 10-minute acquisition times or gaps.

Recommended change: Return sample timestamps or a timestamped history generation from HAL. Update last-reading time only when an acquisition arrives; bin actual timestamps and explicitly represent gaps and reboot retention boundaries.

Verification/limits: checks/health/checks.json/hr_cache_observation: extracted actual heart_tick with one unchanged history byte advanced bpm_last_epoch from1792497600 to1792498200 when only wall-clock time moved600 seconds.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:215 — hist_push stores only a BPM byte; no timestamp is retained.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:291 — First background collection is scheduled two minutes after boot, rather than aligned to local ten-minute slots.
  - nos/svc.c:536 — heart_tick fetches the existing ring; 560 calculates last epoch from current ep and array position.
  - nos/svc.c:561 — Any later calculated epoch replaces bpm_last_epoch, even if no history sample arrived.
  - nos/apps/fit_heart.c:944 — Widget formats age from last_epoch and labels it JUST NOW for age<90 seconds at 947.

HEALTH-005 | P2 | Closing Heart or Breathe can disable HR still requested by a background workout
================================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: strong_inference | domain: health_sports

Trigger: An active workout is sampling HR at up to1Hz while the user leaves Heart or completes/leaves Breathe. The external task observes enabled=0 before the next workout enable, and no background collection is active.

Impact: The strap service can stop the sensor and reset its DSP on restart even though the workout still needs it, creating avoidable HR coverage/reacquisition gaps. The exact interruption duration depends on task timing; it was not measured on hardware.

Recommended change: Use per-consumer leases for workout, Heart, Breathe and background HR. Releasing one lease should not stop a sensor needed by another; log transitions with active owner bits and reason.

Verification/limits: Reachable source schedule verified. No task-interleaving or physical HR gap measurement was performed; assessed as strong inference rather than an observed device failure.

Source evidence:

  - nos/workout.c:279 — Each workout heart sampling call enables the single shared stream; regular metrics are SAMPLE_MS=1000.
  - nos/apps/fit_heart.c:549 — A normal Heart leave calls hal_hr_enable(0) regardless of an active workout.
  - nos/apps/fit_breathe.c:164 — hr_stop calls the same global disable; session completion invokes it at332.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:296 — hal_hr_enable overwrites one enabled flag with no owner or reference count.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:232 — want is enabled||bg;234 stops a running sensor if false;233 restarts when wanted again.

HEALTH-006 | P2 | Energy-family screens reset context on uncover and Quick Report emits an extra mark
================================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: A sheet, pushed child screen or other cover is closed, causing nos call_reveal to invoke the underlying Energy-family enter callback. This includes Energy food/water entry or a partially configured Quick Report.

Impact: Date, zoom, selection and unsaved editor/report choices are discarded. Quick Report also writes another unlabelled time mark on each uncover, so one visit can create unintended duplicate markers.

Recommended change: Honor reveal semantics and retain per-screen editing/timeline state. Separate first-open actions from uncover callbacks, and emit an automatic time mark only for an explicit new report session.

Verification/limits: checks/health/energy_reenter.json: actual extracted refresh/init/energy_enter/report_enter callbacks. Reenter clears day-4/300..600/editor777 to day0/0..1440/editorclosed/250 and produces a second time mark while resetting report choices. This is callback-level testing, not a complete GUI run.

Source evidence:

  - nos/nos.c:179 — call_reveal sets s_revealing and re-invokes enter.
  - nos/apps/fit_energy.c:38 — init unconditionally memset clears the shared E state and sets defaults.
  - nos/apps/fit_energy.c:43 — Energy/Battery/Logs enter callbacks always init; report_enter46 and tune_enter51 do likewise without nos_revealed guards.
  - nos/apps/fit_energy.c:49 — Every report_enter immediately calls sports_report with outcome -1.

HEALTH-007 | P2 | Wrist Tuning reports defaults restored even when the checked save failed
==========================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: The user taps RESET while hal_store_save_checked fails because storage is unavailable or rejects the write.

Impact: The UI announces DEFAULT THRESHOLDS RESTORED although tuning_adjust returns failure before installing the default configuration; active thresholds remain unchanged.

Recommended change: Branch on the checked return value as the sensitivity controls already do; retain a retryable failure message and display the active configuration revision.
