HEALTH-008 | P2 | Activity DAILY AVG excludes every zero-step day and the displayed current day
===============================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: The rolling seven-day chart contains genuine zero-step days, or today is the only day with steps.

Impact: The value labelled THIS WEEK / DAILY AVG is an average of positive previous-day totals only. A single12000-step prior day plus five genuine zero days reports12000 rather than a six-day2000 average; today can show activity while the average remains empty/zero.

Recommended change: Define and label the intended rolling or calendar window. Track missing-day validity separately from genuine zero and use all valid days in the denominator; explicitly decide whether today participates.

Verification/limits: Exact denominator and labels inspected. Arithmetic example follows the actual loop; no full-app render test or historical zero-day fixture was run.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/fit_activity.c:422 — The loop includes seven bars but425 only sums v!=0 and i<6.
  - nos/apps/fit_activity.c:429 — Denominator is the number of nonzero prior-day totals.
  - nos/apps/fit_activity.c:493 — The visible heading is THIS WEEK,496 DAILY AVG.
  - nos/svc.c:402 — week[0..5] are previous rolling days;403 makes index6 current, not a calendar-week boundary.

HEALTH-009 | P2 | Activity history clears its dirty flag after an unchecked failed save
=======================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: A nosact save at rollover or the ten-minute maintenance interval fails, then no new step/active-minute change marks the data dirty before reset.

Impact: Hourly bins, prior-day totals or active minutes can be lost, and the failed state is no longer scheduled for retry. This caller-level issue is distinct from the storage implementation failures STOR-001..003.

Recommended change: Use the checked result, keep dirty state on failure and retry with bounded backoff. Log key, day, error and last successful checkpoint time, separating persisted and RAM activity status.

Verification/limits: Source-proven unconditional dirty-clear path. No hardware failure injection was performed for nosact; unrelated storage test results are not claimed as this caller test.

Source evidence:

  - nos/svc.c:446 — act_save calls void hal_store_save at448, then always clears s_as_dirty and advances the save time at449-450.
  - nos/svc.c:466 — Day rollover uses that unchecked save.
  - nos/svc.c:495 — Periodic saves are gated on s_as_dirty, so a failure with no later change is not retried.
  - core/hal.h:53 — The storage API exposes hal_store_save_checked for callers needing a verified result.

HEALTH-010 | P2 | One workout writer allocation or task-creation failure disables Sports storage until reboot
================================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: At first initialization, the PSRAM writer-memory allocation or PSRAM-backed task creation fails transiently.

Impact: initialized is already true and io remainsNULL, so future init/tick/storage calls never retry. storage.ready staysfalse, preventing new workouts for the remainder of the boot even if memory later becomes available.

Recommended change: Expose a distinct worker-initialization error, retain retryable state and retry allocation/task creation at bounded intervals or on an explicit action. Do not start a workout until durable storage is ready.

Verification/limits: ESP-only source paths and guards inspected; host tests use static writer memory and cannot validate FreeRTOS allocation failure or recovery.

Source evidence:

  - nos/workout.c:692 — workout_init returns immediately after initialized has been set once.
  - nos/workout.c:698 — Writer memory uses heap_caps_calloc;700 sets initialized=1 before allocation is checked.
  - nos/workout.c:701 — Allocation failure returns with ioNULL.
  - nos/workout.c:703 — Task creation failure increments errors and frees/clears io; no retry path follows.
  - nos/workout.c:648 — Storage service returns when ioNULL;729 start requires storage.ready.

HEALTH-011 | P2 | Empty workout history hides loading, unavailable and corrupt-card states
==========================================================================================

Category: logging | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: The user opens Recent Workouts before asynchronous history load completes, or while no history is loaded because the card is unavailable, unreadable or corrupt.

Impact: The UI says No sessions yet / Finish a Sports session even when it has not established that history is empty. The storage layer preserves corrupt banks and exposes ready/corrupt/available, but that distinction is lost on this page.

Recommended change: Use storage status for loading/unavailable/corrupt/original-card-required states; show empty history only after a successful authoritative load. Offer retry and a concise diagnostic reason.

Verification/limits: Source-traced getter/UI branch with explicit storage guards. No live card removal, corruption injection on device, or GUI replay was performed.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/fit_workout.c:826 — history_refresh reads only the current in-memory count and entries.
  - nos/apps/fit_workout.c:867 — With zero entries, history_draw unconditionally renders No sessions yet at869.
  - nos/workout.h:77 — workout_storage_status exposes ready, available, corrupt, foreign and errors for distinguishing states.
  - nos/workout.c:673 — An unreadable/invalid bank pair can leave load incomplete; storage.corrupt is retained at679.

HOST-001 | P2 | Host tools lack one consistent command/reply and rejected-input recovery contract
================================================================================================

Category: dev_qol | confidence: observed | domain: storage_services

Trigger: USB input rejection or a lost DONE occurs during watchctl, flash checkpointing, SD capability probing or scripted QA.

Impact: Different tools retry, stop or wait for different timeouts; a successful navigation can appear failed, an explicit rejected SD command can wait 60s, and operators need bespoke guard/resynchronization code.

Recommended change: Create one tested transport library with capability negotiation, command IDs, explicit dispatch/result ACKs and per-command idempotence metadata; centralize guarded read-only retries and preserve no-blind-retry behavior for recording/reset/delete actions. Export raw wire diagnostics with redaction.

Verification/limits: Observed previous smoke timed out at HOME and capability reads hit ERR command input. Existing host mocks pass their covered scenarios; audit SD lease test currently fails to compile because its do_get2 mock is stale.

Source evidence:

  - tools/watchctl.py:23 — execute uses uncorrelated DONE completion.
  - tools/sdsync.py:190 — GET retries TimeoutError but not typed command-input rejection.
  - tools/sdsync.py:204 — Capability probing can raise on an explicit ERR response.
  - tools/flash_watch.py:9 — Checkpoint logic is separately implemented without a shared reply guard.

OBS-001 | P2 | Persistent crash records do not preserve the faulting instruction, task or backtrace
================================================================================================

Category: logging | confidence: code_proven | domain: storage_services

Trigger: A panic/watchdog reset occurs and the USB serial log was not captured.

Impact: The next boot identifies a crash and replays recent actions, but cannot attribute the actual exception or stack to an app/worker. This limits diagnosis of the reported recording and Battery History crashes.

Recommended change: Add a bounded crash artifact containing build ID, reset cause, exception PC/address, core/task, SP and a supported backtrace/core-dump format. Reserve retention separately from ordinary journal rotation and export symbolized reports using the matching ELF.

Verification/limits: Source/schema/layout review. Two prior panic boots were recorded during debug activities; their exact causes were not established. Do not re-label all historical boot IDs as crashes.

Source evidence:

  - nos/diag_log.h:9 — Defined persistent events store reset context and three integers but no crash payload/core dump record.
  - nos/diag_log.c:146 — Boot capture stores reset reason and recent RTC events.
  - firmware/partitions.csv:1 — Current layout has no dedicated crash/core-dump partition.

OBS-002 | P2 | Allocation diagnostics lack timestamps and allocating callsite context
=====================================================================================

Category: logging | confidence: observed | domain: storage_services

Merged duplicate observations: PLAT-018

Trigger: A transient internal/DMA allocation fails between sparse snapshots.

Impact: NOSALLOC retains only the latest size/caps/function label and counters. Function heap_caps_malloc identifies the allocator rather than the requesting subsystem, so later free-memory reports cannot explain the failure.

Recommended change: Use a small allocation-failure ring in internal/RTC memory with monotonic timestamp, core/task ID, bounded return address/callsite token, caps and nearby free/largest metrics captured only when safe. Persist/translate outside the hook; track resource owner high-water marks.

Verification/limits: Observed last flash diagnostics contain over 1,000 repeated size512 caps0x8 failures but no time/caller. Hook changes must preserve ISR/cache-disabled safety and be verified on Xtensa.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/nos_heap_diag.c:63 — Hook records sequence, size, capability and API function string only.
  - nos/diag_log.c:208 — Periodic heap sample covers general internal heap every 30s, without DMA-specific minimum or failure correlation.

OBS-003 | P2 | Diagnostic evidence shares the failing SD resource and can overflow before recovery
================================================================================================

Category: logging | confidence: observed | domain: storage_services

Trigger: SD allocation/I/O failures stop the sports writer and the diagnostic consumer for minutes.

Impact: The 128-record diagnostic queue fills and drops new records; a 32-entry RTC ring retains only a recent suffix. Action/power history around a prolonged incident can disappear, just when it is needed most.

Recommended change: Prioritize fault/power/recording-transition events over routine events, reserve an emergency bounded channel independent of SD, record first/last/count summaries for repeated failures, and expose a sticky evidence-loss indication with an export bundle.

Verification/limits: Observed pre-flash NOSLOG showed available0, queue128, >500 dropped and >4,000 storage errors. Current flash recovered this temporarily; longer-term retention behavior is not fixed.

Source evidence:

  - nos/diag_log.c:40 — QUEUE_CAP128 and RETAIN_CAP32 bound retention.
  - nos/diag_log.c:136 — Overflow drops new queue events.
  - nos/sports.c:231 — Diagnostic service is called from the shared sports writer worker.
  - nos/diag_log.c:301 — Persistence requires the same SD lease/lock.

PLAT-003 | P2 | Concurrent getter renewal can immediately expire an external sensor lease
=========================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: The ext task captures now, then the UI renews last_get at a later millisecond before the task tests that lease.

Impact: Unsigned now-last_get becomes nearly UINT32_MAX and passes the 8-second idle test. GPS, LTE, or HR can stop while a visible app or workout is renewing them; subsequent enables may restart them. A slow preceding service widens the timing window.

Recommended change: Use a wrap-safe signed elapsed comparison that treats a renewal newer than the captured time as fresh; synchronize lease state and refresh the service timestamp around potentially slow work.

Verification/limits: Actual ext_idle/ext_due helper text was extracted unchanged and compiled. now=10000,last=10001 produced idle=1 although a renewed lease requires 0. Ordinary fresh, expired, and rollover controls behaved as expected; see checks/platform/lease_edge.json.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/ext/ext_sys.c:65 — One timestamp is captured before all seven sequential services.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_priv.h:55 — ext_idle uses unsigned (now-last)>NOS_EXT_IDLE_MS.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_gps.c:251 — GPS stops on that idle result; getter renews last_get at line 301.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:259 — LTE want_on uses the same helper; getters renew last_get at lines 407/420.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:225 — HR disables its user stream on this result; getter renews at line 306.

PLAT-004 | P2 | Heart-rate background sampling has no effective timeout when FIFO acquisition fails
================================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: A background MAX30102 measurement starts, then its FIFO remains empty, FIFO payload reads fail, or repeated pointer reads fail.

Impact: The advertised 40-second background limit is checked only after a successful nonempty FIFO read. The LED stream can remain requested indefinitely; pointer failures call stop but leave bg set, causing another start. Old cached readings can also remain visible to callers that do not inspect sample_ms. Workout metrics explicitly check the 3-second freshness limit, reducing that consumer impact.

Recommended change: Apply the background deadline before any acquisition early return, cap restart attempts, clear acquisition state on persistent errors, and invalidate stale cached outputs consistently.

Verification/limits: Reachable state-machine review; existing host suite does not inject MAX30102 FIFO or bus faults. Measure LED current and recovery on hardware with a controlled sensor fault.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:227 — Background flag is set by the periodic scheduler.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:233 — A retained bg flag restarts a stopped device.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:240 — Pointer failures stop after ten errors but do not clear bg.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:245 — FIFO payload failure returns before timeout evaluation.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:267 — BG_MAX_MS and contact-loss checks are inside the successful nonempty FIFO branch.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:303 — Getter copies cache without age invalidation.
  - nos/workout.c:280 — Workout accepts HR only with quality/range and sample_ms freshness gates.

PLAT-005 | P2 | Environment cache remains valid through repeated conversion-trigger failures
============================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: The BME/BMP driver has a valid sample, then forced-mode trigger writes fail repeatedly, or reads repeatedly return the skipped/not-ready sentinel.

Impact: The old pressure, temperature and humidity remain valid indefinitely. Weather/altimeter UI and logs can present frozen measurements as current; no acquisition timestamp is in hal_env.

Recommended change: Track last successful acquisition and invalidate by age across trigger, readiness and data-read failure paths; expose the last operation/error and retry count.

Verification/limits: Source-proven retention path; no sensor fault injection performed.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/ext/ext_env.c:128 — Trigger-write failures increment errors and return without invalidating cur.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_env.c:140 — Only a later data-read failure branch invalidates after the error threshold.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_env.c:146 — Skipped conversions also return without invalidation or age tracking.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_env.c:163 — Getter returns the cached validity without an age check.

PLAT-006 | P2 | Light cache can remain valid when measurement-channel reads cease succeeding
============================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: APDS status reads continue succeeding, but ALS data reads fail or never become ready after an earlier valid reading.

Impact: The error counter resets after the status read, then the previous valid lux is republished. Automatic brightness can remain based on a frozen reading indefinitely. Proximity has the same missing acquisition-age distinction.

Recommended change: Track ALS/proximity success timestamps and channel errors independently, expire old channels, and expose stale/invalid state to automatic brightness.

Verification/limits: Source review; APDS hardware disconnect/channel-fault behavior remains untested.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/ext/ext_light.c:187 — Only status-read failures advance the invalidation threshold.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_light.c:191 — Status success clears errors before channel reads.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_light.c:196 — Failed/not-ready ALS data preserves the copied cache.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_light.c:217 — Copied validity is republished regardless of fresh channels.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:516 — Automatic brightness trusts hal_light_get and l.valid.

PLAT-007 | P2 | Power-off does not coordinate shutdown of optional strap devices
================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: strong_inference | domain: platform

Trigger: The user confirms POWER OFF while optional GPS, LTE, HR or audio streams are active.

Impact: The ESP enters deep sleep after stopping the board task and onboard IMU, but external device shutdown commands and file-close services have no completion barrier. VBAT-powered strap devices can continue consuming power after the UI says power off. Exact residual current depends on attached hardware and GPIO behavior and was not measured. Audio file ownership is also being reviewed independently by root.

Recommended change: Add an asynchronous platform shutdown coordinator: reject new work, stop each external stream, wait for owned files and hardware acknowledgments with bounded failure reporting, then unmount and sleep.

Verification/limits: Call-chain review and driver power model; validate shutdown under each attached module and measure final rail current.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:561 — Shutdown only checks the IMU recorder and IMU browser.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:580 — Then calls board_prepare_sleep and esp_deep_sleep_start.
  - firmware/main/board.c:642 — Preparation stops board/assets, unmounts SD and disables the onboard IMU; no external shutdown hook.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_gps.c:70 — GPS backup requires an explicit UART command; no power gate wire.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:334 — LTE power-off is an asynchronous multi-step AT/key state machine.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:112 — HR shutdown is an explicit register write from the ext service.

PLAT-008 | P2 | A transient touch I2C failure becomes an input release
======================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: A finger is down during a temporary FT3168 I2C timeout or bus error.

Impact: The read returns the same value as no touch. touch_poll enqueues an up event and a later successful read starts another down event, splitting a drag or completing a tap while the finger is still on the panel. Device evidence contains I2C errors, but it does not prove which gestures were affected.

Recommended change: Make touch acquisition tri-state (valid down/up, invalid read), retain contact briefly across invalid reads, and cancel a gesture explicitly after a bounded fault window rather than emitting a normal tap release.

Verification/limits: Source-proven event transformation. Existing timeout injection and device error evidence are available; no new device interaction was performed.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/board.c:215 — FT3168 board_touch_read returns 0 for both failed I2C and no-contact data.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:433 — touch_poll treats the return directly as down.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:436 — Every down-state change queues a real input sample.
  - firmware/main/board.c:97 — Actual touch driver errors are counted and the last code retained.

PLAT-009 | P2 | Transient boot probe failure disables base touch or IMU until reboot
====================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: The 1.43-B touch probe/mode write or IMU ID/configuration transaction fails once at startup, then the device/bus becomes usable.

Impact: s_touch_ok/s_imu_ok remain false; normal reads return absent without retry. Touch may be unusable for the whole boot, and walking/wrist wake/raw recording remain unavailable. Optional external hot-plug support is separately a missing feature, not assumed to be promised.

Recommended change: Use bounded backoff reprobe/reconfiguration for essential base devices, expose a retry control and first/last error, and avoid spinning/log flooding on genuine absence.

Verification/limits: Code path review; base-board delayed-power-up and bus recovery require hardware validation.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/board.c:606 — Touch probe plus mode write establish a one-time availability flag.
  - firmware/main/board.c:609 — IMU probe/ID/configuration establish a one-time flag.
  - firmware/main/board.c:218 — False touch availability short-circuits all normal reads.
  - firmware/main/board.c:235 — False IMU availability short-circuits raw reads.
  - firmware/main/board.c:530 — Board sampler only enters acquisition when s_imu_ok.

PLAT-010 | P2 | RTC synchronization request is discarded before the write succeeds
==================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: USB/BLE/NTP sets system time, then one RTC control read, stop command, calendar write, or restart fails.

Impact: board_task clears s_rtc_dirty before invoking a void rtc_save. Failure is not requeued, so battery-backed RTC may retain old time or invalid state until another sync or explicit sleep attempt. Early control-read/stop failures return without the RTC warning or validity update.

Recommended change: Return write status, retain the pending request until success with bounded retry, use current trusted UTC when retrying, and log the failed register operation and retry/recovery event.

Verification/limits: Source-proven loss of retry state. Portable RTC encode/decode tests passed; I2C write-failure recovery is not covered.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/board.c:455 — Clock-change callback sets pending epoch and dirty.
  - firmware/main/board.c:468 — Control-read and stop failures return silently.
  - firmware/main/board.c:474 — Calendar/restart failure can mark RTC invalid, but caller receives no result.
  - firmware/main/board.c:575 — Dirty is cleared before rtc_save and never restored on failure.

PLAT-011 | P2 | Wi-Fi scan selection loses long network names
=============================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: The selected network has a 24-32-byte SSID.

Impact: The scan model stores only 23 visible bytes and that truncated value becomes the actual connect target. Scan selection cannot join the full-name network; Other network/phone setup can provide all 32 bytes.

Recommended change: Store the complete 32-byte SSID plus terminator in the HAL snapshot, preserve exact bytes for connection, and truncate only the drawn label.

Verification/limits: Source-proven contract truncation; no network join was attempted.

Source evidence:

  - core/hal.h:87 — hal_wifi_ap contains ssid[24].
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1313 — Full scanned SSID is snprintf-truncated into that field.
  - nos/apps/set_connectivity.c:92 — Password flow copies the truncated scan name.
  - nos/apps/set_connectivity.c:187 — Connect submits the copied name.

PLAT-012 | P2 | Bluetooth off preference changes the tile without disabling the radio
=====================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: The user turns the Bluetooth tile off or boots with nos_prefs.ble=0.

Impact: The saved/tile state changes, but NimBLE still starts and remains connectable. The control therefore cannot deliver the battery or availability behavior implied by off. Current source intentionally keeps the phone link alive, which should be represented honestly in the UI.

Recommended change: Implement explicit BLE enable/disable ownership and restart behavior, or replace the off toggle with a truthful supported phone-link action.

Verification/limits: End-to-end source review; parent coordinates duplicate UI findings. No live radio state was changed.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/sys_control.c:172 — Bluetooth tile state is nos_prefs.ble.
  - nos/apps/sys_control.c:225 — Toggle only changes the preference.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:1502 — Host explicitly keeps BLE up and only applies the Wi-Fi preference.
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1884 — Radio startup always invokes ble_init.
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:682 — BLE connection status ignores the saved enable flag.

PLAT-013 | P2 | Synchronous SD work shares tasks with motion and optional sensor servicing
==========================================================================================

Category: performance | confidence: strong_inference | domain: platform

Trigger: An SD operation blocks for longer than a normal acquisition period: board log/stat/close, storage retry, pedometer save, or audio_service playback/recording I/O.

Impact: Base walking/knock/cached wake and power sampling pause behind board-task SD work. All slow strap services pause behind audio_service, risking GPS UART loss and HR FIFO overflow. The separate raw IMU sampler/writer separation protects its own stream from SD, but does not protect the board cached-motion pipeline. Delay frequency and magnitude have not been measured here.

Recommended change: Move filesystem operations to a dedicated bounded worker, retain mount leases/SD locking, and add loop-gap/max-I/O-duration/FIFO-overrun/serial-loss counters before drawing causal conclusions.

Verification/limits: Conditional source analysis. Existing tests verify portable algorithms, not slow-card scheduling. Replay on a controlled delayed I/O backend before hardware tests.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/board.c:527 — Mount retry is synchronous in the same board loop as sensing.
  - firmware/main/board.c:569 — Pedometer save and periodic board log occur before the next sensing cycle.
  - firmware/main/board.c:443 — Board log uses synchronous fopen/fprintf/fclose and storage_status.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_sys.c:66 — audio_service runs before GPS/LTE/environment/light/mag/HR on one task.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_audio.c:349 — Audio playback reads SD on that service task; recording fwrite is at line 371.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_gps.c:265 — Driver notes the 512-byte UART ring fills in about 44ms at 115200.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_hr.c:241 — HR hardware FIFO has 32 samples at the configured 100Hz effective rate.

PLAT-014 | P2 | Display DMA waits have no bounded fault or recovery path
========================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: The display driver accepts a submission but an expected completion callback never returns a credit, or the flush worker stops progressing.

Impact: Raw/rotated credit waits and the UI frame fence can block indefinitely, freezing UI commands, preference pumping and the UI-owned service loop. Other FreeRTOS tasks may continue. This is a recovery gap, not a claim that it caused a historical hang.

Recommended change: Add bounded progress monitoring and a deferred diagnostic snapshot containing queue/credit/job state; recover through an owned display reinitialization or controlled restart after preserving settings. Never return buffer ownership while DMA might still be active.

Verification/limits: Source-proven unbounded waits conditional on a missing completion. Frame-timer failure tests passed but do not cover DMA loss.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:191 — Final DMA fence takes two credits with portMAX_DELAY.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:233 — Flush worker waits for DMA credits indefinitely.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:317 — UI raster waits for raw credit indefinitely.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:336 — frame_drain waits indefinitely for worker fence.
  - firmware/main/display_bench.c:95 — Separate benchmark code has an explicit DMA timeout; active NOS path does not.

PLAT-015 | P2 | Panel command failures are ignored after host state is advanced
===============================================================================

Category: logging | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: An invert, brightness, sleep or wake DCS transaction returns an error.

Impact: Software assumes the state change succeeded and may suppress future commands as redundant. A failed brightness or wake command can leave the physical panel out of sync with power/theme state, without a retained operation error or retry. Bitmap submission uses ESP_ERROR_CHECK, so error policy is inconsistent.

Recommended change: Record each panel command result and actual acknowledgment, update applied state only on success, and add bounded retry/recovery with last failed operation/register.

Verification/limits: Source review; no panel fault injection. Existing bitmap/rotation tests do not exercise DCS errors.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:196 — Invert/sleep/wake/brightness branch return values are ignored through line 213.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:263 — Theme state is advanced before command completion.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:505 — Brightness cache is advanced before the transaction.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:531 — Sleep state is advanced before transaction completion.

PLAT-016 | P2 | Air analyzer reports success if its lease/hop timer fails
=========================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: Timer allocation or periodic start fails while entering the Wi-Fi air analyzer.

Impact: The station link is already parked and promiscuous mode enabled, but the function still marks the analyzer on and returns success. Without timer callbacks it cannot hop channels or enforce the 3-second auto-release contract; station reconnection requires a separate stop path.

Recommended change: Check timer creation/start, undo promiscuous mode and restore station intent on every failed entry, and keep an independent supervisor for lease expiry.

Verification/limits: Source-proven failure path; host test suite has no analyzer timer fault injection.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1754 — Station intent is parked before timer creation.
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1772 — esp_timer_create result is ignored.
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1774 — Periodic start result is ignored, then on=1 and success are returned.
  - firmware/main/hal_esp32.c:1722 — Auto-release only executes from the timer callback.

PLAT-020 | P2 | GPS can invalidate a newly parsed fix using the older service-loop timestamp
============================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: gps_service receives now from loop entry, then pump parses a valid sentence at a later millisecond.

Impact: sentence records a newer last_fix timestamp, but the post-pump stale check subtracts it from the older unsigned now. The elapsed value wraps and the same pass clears the newly valid fix. Physical frequency depends on task/I/O timing and has not been measured.

Recommended change: Refresh the monotonic timestamp after pump before stale checks, and use a wrap-safe elapsed helper that treats newer acquisition timestamps as fresh. Apply the same rule to lease and data timestamps across services.

Verification/limits: Exact source ordering and unsigned arithmetic reviewed; the extracted-helper renewal check demonstrates the same ordering class, but no GPS parser/hardware replay was performed.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/ext/ext_sys.c:65 — Loop captures now before earlier services and passes it to gps_service.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_gps.c:147 — sentence obtains fresh ext_ms for fix acquisition.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_gps.c:158 — Valid GGA records last_fix=now; RMC does so at line 167.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_gps.c:250 — G_RUN pumps NMEA before checking freshness.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_gps.c:260 — Uses old caller now minus new G->last_fix, with unsigned elapsed comparison.

PROTO-001 | P2 | Notification text can be mistaken for a clock or timezone command
==================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: storage_services

Trigger: A normal notification contains a documentation example such as setTime(...) or E.setTimeZone(...) within its quoted body.

Impact: The frame parser recognizes both the notification JSON and the text inside it as a clock command. svc applies the clock/timezone before posting the notification, potentially shifting alarms, dates and logs.

Recommended change: Parse top-level command framing lexically and ignore command-like strings inside JSON/quoted text. Add round-trip tests using benign notification examples and multiple framed commands.

Verification/limits: Positive host regression against actual gb.c: checks/protocol/notification_clock.log. No phone or watch traffic was generated.

Source evidence:

  - nos/gb.c:89 — Clock command is found by substring search over the entire line.
  - nos/gb.c:94 — Timezone command is also searched inside quoted content.
  - nos/svc.c:1082 — Any extracted time fields are applied before JSON handling.

STOR-005 | P2 | Storage read failures are reported as missing files
===================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: storage_services

Trigger: fopen fails because of low DMA memory, exhausted handles, permission errors, or I/O errors rather than a nonexistent path.

Impact: SDGET/SDGET2/SDCRC return MISSING; host raises FileNotFoundError and deliberately avoids retry. Operators cannot tell absence from the failure mode that blocked the previous flash.

Recommended change: Reserve MISSING for ENOENT; return a typed operation/error/errno response for other failures. Keep retries bounded and read-only; include SD generation and resource telemetry in failure reports.

Verification/limits: Source-backed reachable condition. Prior flash evidence checkpoint-read-2.log includes unreadable slot 0 while allocation failures were occurring; do not infer permanent file absence from that message.

Source evidence:

  - firmware/main/sd_xfer.c:104 — Legacy GET maps any fopen failure to MISSING.
  - firmware/main/sd_xfer.c:180 — Paced GET has the same mapping.
  - firmware/main/sd_xfer.c:248 — CRC query also maps any fopen failure to MISSING.
  - tools/sdsync.py:220 — Host treats MISSING as a missing file, not a transient transport/storage failure.

STOR-007 | P2 | Retried journal records can bias on-watch battery-history averages
==================================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: strong_inference | domain: storage_services

Trigger: A failed close/write batch already appended complete records, then retries the same boot/sequence IDs; battery-history query scans both copies.

Impact: Intake records are deduplicated, but battery samples are counted twice, giving repeated samples extra weight and overstating sample counts. Offline exporters and on-watch summaries can disagree.

Recommended change: Deduplicate all journal records by durable identity before aggregation, or commit batches with durable replay-safe boundaries. Carry last-seen identity per stream where append ordering permits a bounded implementation.

Verification/limits: Call-chain/code review; no new hardware reproduction. Existing recovered archives contain repeated records and exporters explicitly deduplicate them; a dedicated failed-close replay aggregation regression should be added.

Source evidence:

  - nos/sports.c:219 — Failed batches retain queue IDs specifically so replays can be identified.
  - nos/sports.c:346 — bin_record accumulates every battery sample.
  - nos/sports.c:420 — Async query deduplicates FOOD/WATER only.
  - nos/sports.c:456 — Synchronous query has the same special case.

APP-019 | P3 | Revealing Torch after an alert resets red/dim mode to full white
===============================================================================

Category: edge_case | confidence: code_proven | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Select red Torch or lower the white light level, let a call/alarm/Find Watch cover the screen, and dismiss it.

Recommended change: Preserve Torch's mode and per-mode level across covers/reveals, initialize defaults only on a new opening, and reapply the preserved override after the covering screen restores brightness.

APP-020 | P3 | Critical time/connection actions lack result and transition diagnostics
======================================================================================

Category: logging | confidence: code_proven | domain: content_apps

Trigger: Investigate a missed alarm, nested alert, disappearing phone link, failed SMS, rejected GPS point, or Recorder save error after the fact.

Impact: Generic navigation records identify screen entry but not event generation, action request, backend completion, stale age, rejected point reason, or persistence outcome. Track/Recorder emit a coarse SP_ROUTINE value; LTE/audio expose aggregate debug counters, making a specific failure difficult to reconstruct.

Recommended change: Add bounded transition-only diagnostic events with request/session generation, old/new state, source, age/reason, and completion status. Rate-limit aggregate rejected-fix counters; keep message bodies/caller content out of routine logs.

Verification/limits: method: Reviewed log/printf/sports_emit call sites in every scoped app and the corresponding service/HAL paths.

Source evidence:

  - nos/nos.c:176 — Generic logs record screen name/depth/reveal only.
  - nos/svc.c:743 — Alarm matching, firing, timeout, and persistence path has no dedicated event/result log.
  - nos/svc.c:797 — Timer actions likewise expose no structured action/state/result events.
  - nos/svc_track.c:55 — Rejected GPS fixes have no counter/reason/event.
  - nos/apps/con_memo.c:318 — The coarse stop routine is emitted before final save completion is known.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_lte.c:328 — Failure is retained only as an aggregate count rather than a request result.

DEV-014 | P3 | Private worktree and temporary-output policy is not encoded in repository tooling
================================================================================================

Category: dev_qol | confidence: code_proven | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Create the required workspace-local .worktrees checkout/temp directory, then run status/add-all or concurrent simulator/test jobs with default settings.

Impact: .gitignore covers build/dist/firmware outputs but not .worktrees; git check-ignore returns no match for .worktrees/_audit_tmp/check.tmp. Build verification uses the ambient TEMP directory and fixed child name, and simulator named binaries share objects. An isolated audit/dev checkout can pollute status or place heavy temporary files on C:, and concurrent output provenance is unclear. This audit explicitly redirects TEMP/TMP to A: and writes checks separately.

Recommended change: Document and enforce workspace-local .worktrees plus A:-resident TEMP/TMP for heavy runs; ignore that private root, validate resolved output/delete targets and use GUID run directories or cache locks with manifests. Add disk-space/output-root preflight to heavy jobs.

Verification/limits: git check-ignore .worktrees/_audit_tmp/check.tmp returned1/no match. No worktree or recursive move/delete was performed.

Source evidence:

  - .gitignore:1 — Ignore list includes build and firmware artifacts but no workspace worktree directory.
  - sim/nos_build.ps1:48 — Named outputs share one object directory.
  - sim/nos_build.ps1:119 — Verify output relies on ambient TEMP and fixed basename.
  - tools/test_workouts.ps1:10 — Existing GUID output pattern demonstrates a suitable local isolation approach.

DEV-016 | P3 | Connectivity hardware QA opens COM3 and performs mutations at module import
==========================================================================================

Category: dev_qol | confidence: code_proven | domain: developer_tooling

Trigger: Import tools/check_connectivity_device.py for helper discovery/testing, or execute it on a host where COM3 is a different target.

Impact: The module opens a hardcoded serial port at top level, then changes watch UI/preferences, joins a Windows WLAN profile and invokes HardReset during cleanup, even when an earlier assertion fails after original preferences were read. There is no CLI --port/identity/dry-run boundary or __main__ guard. Existing restoration logic is useful but cannot make import read-only or verify that COM3 is the intended watch.

Recommended change: Move execution into main(), require an explicit port and target identity, separate offline helpers from device scenarios, and describe the exact mutations/reset in --help. Keep restoration in a robust finally block and retain proof of the prior/current configuration.

Verification/limits: Source/AST-level review only. This module was not imported, executed, or allowed to access COM3.

Source evidence:

  - tools/check_connectivity_device.py:20 — Hardcoded COM3 and top-level port.open24.
  - tools/check_connectivity_device.py:90 — Top-level try begins the active network/settings scenario.
  - tools/check_connectivity_device.py:113 — Creates/connects a Windows WLAN profile.
  - tools/check_connectivity_device.py:177 — Cleanup performs HardReset once original preferences exist, followed by UI restoration.

ENG-004 | P3 | Generic action/navigation records cannot reconstruct selected UI targets or action completion
================================================================================================

Category: logging | confidence: code_proven | domain: engine

Trigger: Export diagnostics after a wrong action, interrupted transition, Control Center toggle, picker choice or notification dismissal.

Impact: Logs can identify a screen and input event, but cannot establish which card/tile/row/face was intended, which ID was acted on, or whether navigation completed/cancelled. This particularly obstructs diagnosing ENG-001. Screen-name hash decoding does exist in tools/diag_export.py, so the gap is payload semantics, not a missing decoder.

Recommended change: Emit sparse semantic records at action commit/completion with stable target ID and result, plus source/destination/transition outcome for navigation. Keep high-rate raw touch streams separate from these finite domain events.

Verification/limits: Static producer/caller/exporter trace; no claim of dropped log transport. Validate by replaying a known card-dismiss and toggle sequence and resolving intent/result without screenshots.

Source evidence:

  - nos/nos.c:176 — Navigation record carries hashed screen name,depth,revealing flag only.
  - nos/nos.c:689 — Touch action record carries hashed screen name,event type,axis; no target coordinates/ID/result.
  - nos/nos.c:889 — Button action similarly stores screen hash and event type.
  - nos/apps/sys_notifs.c:205 — Dismissal acts without a semantic diagnostic record of intended/current notification ID.
  - nos/apps/sys_control.c:218 — Toggle implements multiple distinct actions with no tile/value domain event.
  - tools/diag_export.py:75 — Existing exporter builds name-ID decoding; avoid duplicating that capability.

ENG-005 | P3 | Engine timing and ink-damage API comments describe superseded behavior
=====================================================================================

Category: dev_qol | confidence: code_proven | domain: engine

Trigger: Implement or debug a screen using the current nos.h contract, especially dt-dependent animation or a stationary ink-dim fade.

Impact: The header promises whole-period quantization and says ink dim is absent from damage hashes, whereas current implementation uses bounded exact elapsed dt and hashes dim. Implementers can introduce unnecessary manual markers or make incorrect timing assumptions.

Recommended change: Update nos.h to the exact bounded elapsed-time contract, distinguish authored frame sampling from engine dt, and document current dim tracking/callback requirements. Keep API documentation aligned with the current regression tests.

Verification/limits: Direct header/implementation/test comparison; no behavior change or production edit performed.

Source evidence:

  - nos/nos.h:6 — Header describes nos_dt as a whole number of frame periods; frame-run contract repeats quantization at line257.
  - nos/nos.c:2236 — Current implementation explicitly follows elapsed time, using elapsed_ms/1000 at line2241.
  - nos/nos.h:159 — Ink-dim comment states dim-only changes are not repainted.
  - core/gfx.c:29 — gfx_rec_cmd hashes fb->dim into the command signature.
  - tests/nos_engine_clock_test.c:59 — Existing regression exercises irregular12/28/8/25ms intervals instead of quantized periods.

FACE-011 | P3 | Shared date fades retain a30-Hz timebase under the configured40-Hz cadence
==========================================================================================

Category: bug | confidence: code_proven | domain: faces

Trigger: A midnight/date-label change cross-fades on any ordinary face using fk_xfade with the default NOS_FPS40 build.

Impact: The stated five-frame fade takes about seven40-Hz updates and repeats some perceptual levels instead of one level per nominal frame. The shared blink also retains30-fps phase constants, so its documented500ms hold is375ms at40Hz.

Recommended change: Use the configured cadence consistently for counted fades; express intended real-time blink milestones through named duration conversions if500ms is the product requirement.

Verification/limits: Source timebase comparison, not a physical judder claim. Card dt*30 rate scaling was separately noted but is not automatically classified as a malfunction.

Source evidence:

  - nos/render_config.h:6 — Default NOS_FPS is40; M_FPS follows it.
  - nos/faces/faces.c:140 — XF_HZ remains30.
  - nos/faces/faces.c:157 — Fade index uses elapsed seconds times XF_HZ.
  - nos/faces/faces.c:72 — Other face animation frames use M_FPS.
  - nos/faces/faces.c:801 — Blink uses fk_sec_k/M_FPS with old15/18 step thresholds.

HEALTH-012 | P3 | Frozen wrist-placement provenance is only a volatile UI flag
==============================================================================

Category: dev_qol | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: A guided wrist suggestion is confirmed and the system reboots, or the user subsequently changes watch wrist in general Settings rather than Sports setup.

Impact: Persisted wrist/gesture references survive, but placement_source resets to USER after reboot or can remain CALIBRATED after an unrelated manual wrist change. Later workout metadata can misstate how the chosen wrist was established.

Recommended change: Persist explicit wrist-selection source and calibration version with the selected wrist, or conservatively tag all subsequent manually selected sessions USER. Invalidate provenance consistently through a shared preference setter.

Verification/limits: Preference layout, both manual setters and guided setter traced. No claim that a guided suggestion establishes anatomical wrist identity; user confirmation remains required.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/fit_workout.c:36 — placement_source is a file-static initialized to PLACEMENT_USER.
  - nos/apps/fit_workout.c:112 — configure_session freezes that volatile value into each workout.
  - nos/apps/fit_workout.c:745 — Guided confirmation sets CALIBRATED; only the Sports manual toggle resets it at627.
  - nos/prefs.h:36 — Preferences persist hand selections and wrist_refs but no placement provenance/revision.
  - nos/apps/set_settings.c:1703 — General Settings toggles wrist_left without informing the Sports placement_source flag.

HEALTH-013 | P3 | Live IMU graph advertises a fixed4.3-second window without timestamped sampling
================================================================================================

Category: dev_qol | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: The live graph fills its64-entry ring. It samples when at least50ms have passed, with no timestamps attached to individual entries.

Impact: At the nominal20Hz cap the ring holds about3.2seconds, while a30FPS UI commonly accepts a sample every66.7ms, about4.27seconds. Scheduling load can lengthen the span further; the fixed4.3S diagnostic label cannot establish a measured time axis.

Recommended change: Store sample timestamps and label the actual visible duration, or drive the live chart from a declared fixed-rate producer. Keep raw recorder rate/loss metadata separate from this filtered live preview.

Verification/limits: Source arithmetic and scheduling dependence inspected. No claim that raw recorder runs at the UI rate; its separate recorder is reviewed by root.

Source evidence:

  - nos/apps/out_imu.c:12 — HIST is64 samples.
  - nos/apps/out_imu.c:49 — Live samples are accepted when wall-clock delta>=50ms, then last resets to actual current time.
  - nos/apps/out_imu.c:61 — Ring increments once per accepted UI poll, not on a fixed producer clock.
  - nos/apps/out_imu.c:115 — Graph key is ACCEL +/-2G /4.3S; no acquisition timestamps are used in trace drawing at92-96.

HEALTH-014 | P3 | Workout quality rejection and writer failures lack retained causal detail
===========================================================================================

Category: logging | confidence: code_proven | domain: health_sports

Trigger: A workout loses HR/GNSS/motion coverage or a checkpoint fails, and the developer later inspects only stored snapshots/status.

Impact: Coverage totals and coarse GPS state show that data is missing, but not which age, quality, saturation, discontinuity or I/O condition caused it. A single storage.errors count cannot distinguish open/read/write/fsync/readback/worker failures. Separate sports raw logs may be absent or share the failed SD path, as root OBS-003 documents.

Recommended change: Add bounded per-session rejection counters, latest failure time/reason and accepted acquisition times. Capture storage operation/slot/errno and worker resource failure separately; persist summaries when storage recovers without flooding sample logs.

Verification/limits: Logging callsites and all quality/error returns inspected. This is an explicit observability gap, not evidence that any specific physical session failed for one of these reasons.

Source evidence:

  - nos/workout.c:280 — HR quality/range/age gates collapse into ok=false and zero live HR at300; no retained per-reason counts.
  - nos/workout.c:312 — GPS exposes a few broad states; jump/finite/range reasons at354/384 are not separately counted.
  - nos/workout_motion.c:13 — Invalid or implausible samples reset boundaries without reason counters.
  - nos/workout.c:681 — All storage failures increment the same errors count, including lock/read/fsync/readback paths.
  - nos/workout.c:741 — Existing DIAG_WORKOUT logs state transitions rather than sensor rejection causes.

PLAT-019 | P3 | Legacy contracts and diagnostic labels misdescribe current hardware and storage behavior
================================================================================================

Category: dev_qol | confidence: code_proven | domain: platform

Trigger: A developer follows retained HAL/OTA comments or reads generic board/ext status output.

Impact: NVS-only storage comments, retired serial OTA instructions, a hardcoded board audio=0, and an ext display_mode cache that the strong NOS override never updates obscure the current implementation. They can mislead debugging and future changes; this is documentation/observability debt, not evidence that the physical hardware is absent or a save failed.

Recommended change: Update comments/help to the active source of truth; expose supported base/strap capabilities and current applied display/radio state in one versioned snapshot. Mark dormant entrypoints and legacy code clearly.

Verification/limits: Source and callsite inspection only; no need for production tests for comment corrections.

Source evidence:

  - core/hal.h:49 — Persistent store comment still describes device NVS, while active implementation is SD-authoritative.
  - firmware/main/ota.h:4 — Advertises serial ota URL/ota sd; active nos_main command dispatch has no corresponding calls.
  - firmware/main/board.c:634 — Generic BOARD record prints audio=0 unconditionally.
  - firmware/main/ext/ext_sys.c:124 — Weak display override alone updates s_display_mode; active NOS strong hal_display_power bypasses it.
  - firmware/main/nos_main.c:88 — C board explicitly rejected, despite comment referring to a legacy-main fallback not selected by current CMake.

CROSS-CUTTING LOGGING SYSTEM
============================

P1 | Common event envelope

Versioned bounded binary records: monotonic timestamp, optional UTC plus validity, boot/reset ID, sequence, build/ELF/source IDs, subsystem, severity, event ID, action/session/request ID, state before/after, result/error, duration and explicit evidence-loss counters. Keep human formatting and symbol resolution on the host.

P1 | Crash and allocation first-fault channel

Keep panic-safe/RTC/internal evidence for reset reason, task/core, fault PC, stack/backtrace or coredump reference, last action/session and allocation request/caps/callsite token with internal/DMA free/largest/minimum. Use safe capture hooks and deferred persistence. Preserve matching ELF and a host symbolizer; do not do file I/O or formatted logging inside unsafe hooks.

P1 | Storage phase and recovery trace

Trace lease acquisition, mount generation, lock wait, operation phase, stable path ID, errno/result, bytes, slot/generation/CRC, queue high-water/drop, dirty age, fsync/readback latency and retry outcome. Log CSV chunk/checkpoint/recovery boundaries, not every IMU sample. Give critical faults a separate reserved budget from regular sensor history.

P1 | Power ownership and measured evidence

Record every power state transition with reason, SOC validity/source, rail sample age, CPU/APB lock owners and held duration, display/brightness, actual radio/strap state, wake source, sleep refusal and recorder/background owners. Summarize periodic residency. A measured current/charge integration run with known battery/USB state is required before claiming consumption or capacity.

P2 | App actions and sensor truth

Begin/result events with stable target IDs for settings changes, alarms/timers, notifications, SMS, memos, workout start/pause/save/recover and sensor calibration. Include sample timestamp/quality/stale reason, source, worn wrist and playing hand/confidence/provenance. Log presentation and acquisition separately to detect stale UI.

P2 | Frame and asynchronous work spans

Use a bounded triggerable frame ring for input-to-present, tick/layout/raster/blur, DMA submit/complete/wait, asset admission/publish/cancel and SD stalls. Capture configuration, moving regions, cache cold/warm, missed floor/deadline and relevant lock/resource owners. Aggregate normal frames; preserve a short pre/post fault window.

P2 | Retention, export and developer controls

Per-module levels with bounded duration, rate limiting/coalescing, queue/drop telemetry and explicit session markers. Export one manifest-linked redacted bundle with logs, settings schema/hash, sensor/profile metadata, trace, crash and matching build artifacts. Default to excluding credentials, phone message text, raw contacts and location; make sensitive data selection explicit.

DEVELOPER QUALITY OF LIFE ROADMAP
=================================

P1 | One validation and coverage command

Run portable unit tests, simulator scenarios, generated asset checks, partition/heap budgets and source/build identity checks with one machine-readable result. Include the currently broken SD lease fixture and record skips and unavailable hardware separately from passes.

P1 | Reusable host command transport

Shared parser/capabilities, correlated commands, explicit accepted/completed results, typed errors, read-only retry policy, mutation idempotence rules and retained redacted wire traces across watchctl, sdsync, flash checkpoints and benchmark helpers.

P1 | Fault injection and replay library

Script missing/removed/slow card, transient EIO/open/short write/fsync failure, allocation rejection, delayed DMA completion, stale/out-of-order samples, clock jumps and alert cover/reveal. Reuse audit repros as future regressions after fixes; positive repros are not passing product correctness tests.

P1 | Build/provenance and symbolication bundle

Save dirty source fingerprint, config, partition map, compiler/IDF, native asset hash, firmware/ELF hashes, symbolizer inputs and recovery metadata per flashed image. Refuse a stale or wrong-workspace image; retain the current NVS-preserving checkpoint workflow.

P2 | Sports evaluation rather than heuristic claims

Version profile/model/calibration, expose worn wrist separately from playing hand, confidence and unknown state. Replay the confirmed right-handed/left-wrist badminton recordings as one known context. Build a held-out bilateral, non-dominant and mixed-sport matrix for football, cricket, badminton, running and hiking before accuracy claims.

P2 | Accessible in-watch diagnostic workflow

Add last-fault summary, current SD/heap/DMA and sensor freshness, active resource owners, unsaved settings, recording-recovery state, a capture marker, bounded trace toggle and one bundle export. Make destructive actions deliberate and report completion/errors instead of silent UI state changes.

P2 | Simulator and device contract comparison

Mark simulated capabilities and estimated costs. Run the same action/session scripts against simulator and device with explicit expectations for hardware absence, timers, persistence, timezones, asynchronous results and raw-data schemas. Maintain app/screen lifecycle coverage, not just screenshots.

VALIDATION AND HARDWARE GAPS
============================

Storage and recovery

Current: 4 positive fault-chain reproductions; existing host storage/protocol tests expose one compile failure.

Next: Physical card removal, power loss at each checkpoint phase, CRC/torn-tail recovery, recording auto-resume and journal replay/dedup.

Rendering and input

Current: Engine/geometry/navigation/blur/asset host suites; inherited target scroll captures.

Next: Long-origin device timing, 30 FPS cold/warm floor per screen, actual touch bus faults, display/DMA recovery and optical trail review.

Sports and health

Current: Heuristic/model/profile/source review and host scenarios; two badminton contexts user-confirmed.

Next: Held-out bilateral sports/wrist data, fresh versus stale HR, recording and history consistency, calibrated sensors and repeated long sessions.

Power and reliability

Current: Historical low-DMA/SD incidents plus one fresh read-only USB snapshot.

Next: Unplugged >=4 h mixed-use and badminton soak with current integration, power state/owner trace, crash capture and evidence-loss assertions.

Optional peripherals and companion

Current: Driver/control-flow and narrow host stubs; actual physical attachments/phone interop not exercised.

Next: Absent/faulty/working strap matrix, GPS outage/reacquisition, modem/SMS result, BLE/Wi-Fi preference effects and reconnect.

Build and tooling

Current: Current source hashes and native asset verification; existing build identity/headroom imported.

Next: Single CI gate, simulator script matrix, build/package hash verification, symbolizer dry run and manifest-linked export round trip.
